| Xiaomi",description:"Introducing the MiMo-V2.6 series: frontier intelligence, all the modalities, built in public."},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimo-v2-6/index.mdx"},{title:"",routePath:"/mimocode",lang:"en",toc:[],frontmatter:{pageType:"blank"},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode.mdx"},{title:"Agents",routePath:"/mimocode/agents",lang:"en",toc:[{text:"Types",id:"types",depth:2,charIndex:442},{text:"Primary agents",id:"primary-agents",depth:3,charIndex:608},{text:"Subagents",id:"subagents",depth:3,charIndex:1193},{text:"Built-in",id:"built-in",depth:2,charIndex:1543},{text:"Use build",id:"use-build",depth:3,charIndex:1715},{text:"Use plan",id:"use-plan",depth:3,charIndex:2e3},{text:"Use general",id:"use-general",depth:3,charIndex:2524},{text:"Use explore",id:"use-explore",depth:3,charIndex:2857},{text:"Use compaction",id:"use-compaction",depth:3,charIndex:3165},{text:"Use title",id:"use-title",depth:3,charIndex:3419},{text:"Use summary",id:"use-summary",depth:3,charIndex:3642},{text:"Usage",id:"usage",depth:2,charIndex:3862},{text:"Configure",id:"configure",depth:2,charIndex:4869},{text:"JSON",id:"json",depth:3,charIndex:5083},{text:"Markdown",id:"markdown",depth:3,charIndex:5228},{text:"Options",id:"options",depth:2,charIndex:5557},{text:"Description",id:"description",depth:3,charIndex:5704},{text:"Temperature",id:"temperature",depth:3,charIndex:5940},{text:"Max steps",id:"max-steps",depth:3,charIndex:6705},{text:"Disable",id:"disable",depth:3,charIndex:7356},{text:"Prompt",id:"prompt",depth:3,charIndex:7486},{text:"Model",id:"model",depth:3,charIndex:7877},{text:"Tools (deprecated)",id:"tools-deprecated",depth:3,charIndex:8492},{text:"Permissions",id:"permissions",depth:3,charIndex:9210},{text:"Mode",id:"mode",depth:3,charIndex:10003},{text:"Hidden",id:"hidden",depth:3,charIndex:10310},{text:"Task permissions",id:"task-permissions",depth:3,charIndex:10774},{text:"Color",id:"color",depth:3,charIndex:11486},{text:"Top P",id:"top-p",depth:3,charIndex:11820},{text:"Additional",id:"additional",depth:3,charIndex:12109},{text:"Create agents",id:"create-agents",depth:2,charIndex:12664},{text:"Use cases",id:"use-cases",depth:2,charIndex:13137},{text:"Examples",id:"examples",depth:2,charIndex:13639},{text:"Documentation agent",id:"documentation-agent",depth:3,charIndex:13847},{text:"Security auditor",id:"security-auditor",depth:3,charIndex:13954}],frontmatter:{title:"Agents",description:"Configure and use specialized agents."},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode/agents.mdx",rawContent:'Agents are specialized AI assistants that can be configured for specific tasks and workflows. They allow you to create focused tools with custom prompts, models, and tool access.\n\n:::tip\nUse the plan agent to analyze code and review suggestions without making any code changes.\n:::\n\nYou can switch between agents during a session or invoke them with the `@` mention.\n\n---\n\n## Types\n\nThere are two types of agents in MiMo Code; primary agents and subagents.\n\n---\n\n### Primary agents\n\nPrimary agents are the main assistants you interact with directly. You can cycle through them using the **Tab** key, or your configured `switch_agent` keybind. These agents handle your main conversation. Tool access is configured via permissions — for example, Build has all tools enabled while Plan is restricted.\n\n:::tip\nYou can use the **Tab** key to switch between primary agents during a session.\n:::\n\nMiMo Code comes with two built-in primary agents, **Build** and **Plan**. We\'ll\nlook at these below.\n\n---\n\n### Subagents\n\nSubagents are specialized assistants that primary agents can invoke for specific tasks. You can also manually invoke them by **@ mentioning** them in your messages.\n\nMiMo Code comes with two built-in subagents, **General** and **Explore**. We\'ll look at this below.\n\n---\n\n## Built-in\n\nMiMo Code comes with two built-in primary agents and two built-in subagents.\n\n---\n\n### Use build\n\n_Mode_: `primary`\n\nBuild is the **default** primary agent with all tools enabled. This is the standard agent for development work where you need full access to file operations and system commands.\n\n---\n\n### Use plan\n\n_Mode_: `primary`\n\nA restricted agent designed for planning and analysis. We use a permission system to give you more control and prevent unintended changes.\nBy default, all of the following are set to `ask`:\n\n- `file edits`: All writes, patches, and edits\n- `bash`: All bash commands\n\nThis agent is useful when you want the LLM to analyze code, suggest changes, or create plans without making any actual modifications to your codebase.\n\n---\n\n### Use general\n\n_Mode_: `subagent`\n\nA general-purpose agent for researching complex questions and executing multi-step tasks. Has full tool access (except todo), so it can make file changes when needed. Use this to run multiple units of work in parallel.\n\n---\n\n### Use explore\n\n_Mode_: `subagent`\n\nA fast, read-only agent for exploring codebases. Cannot modify files. Use this when you need to quickly find files by patterns, search code for keywords, or answer questions about the codebase.\n\n---\n\n### Use compaction\n\n_Mode_: `primary`\n\nHidden system agent that compacts long context into a smaller summary. It runs automatically when needed and is not selectable in the UI.\n\n---\n\n### Use title\n\n_Mode_: `primary`\n\nHidden system agent that generates short session titles. It runs automatically and is not selectable in the UI.\n\n---\n\n### Use summary\n\n_Mode_: `primary`\n\nHidden system agent that creates session summaries. It runs automatically and is not selectable in the UI.\n\n---\n\n## Usage\n\n1. For primary agents, use the **Tab** key to cycle through them during a session. You can also use your configured `switch_agent` keybind.\n\n2. Subagents can be invoked:\n   - **Automatically** by primary agents for specialized tasks based on their descriptions.\n   - Manually by **@ mentioning** a subagent in your message. For example.\n\n     ```txt frame="none"\n     @general help me search for this function\n     ```\n\n3. **Navigation between sessions**: When subagents create child sessions, use `session_child_first` (default: **\\+Down**) to enter the first child session from the parent.\n\n4. Once you are in a child session, use:\n   - `session_child_cycle` (default: **Right**) to cycle to the next child session\n   - `session_child_cycle_reverse` (default: **Left**) to cycle to the previous child session\n   - `session_parent` (default: **Up**) to return to the parent session\n\n   This lets you switch between the main conversation and specialized subagent work.\n\n---\n\n## Configure\n\nYou can customize the built-in agents or create your own through configuration. Agents can be configured in two ways:\n\n---\n\n### JSON\n\nConfigure agents in your `mimocode.json` config file:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "agent": {\n    "build": {\n      "mode": "primary",\n      "model": "xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro",\n      "prompt": "{file:./prompts/build.txt}",\n      "tools": {\n        "write": true,\n        "edit": true,\n        "bash": true\n      }\n    },\n    "plan": {\n      "mode": "primary",\n      "model": "xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro",\n      "tools": {\n        "write": false,\n        "edit": false,\n        "bash": false\n      }\n    },\n    "code-reviewer": {\n      "description": "Reviews code for best practices and potential issues",\n      "mode": "subagent",\n      "model": "xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro",\n      "prompt": "You are a code reviewer. Focus on security, performance, and maintainability.",\n      "tools": {\n        "write": false,\n        "edit": false\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n### Markdown\n\nYou can also define agents using markdown files. Place them in:\n\n- Global: `~/.config/mimocode/agents/`\n- Per-project: `.mimocode/agents/`\n\n```markdown title="~/.config/mimocode/agents/review.md"\n---\ndescription: Reviews code for quality and best practices\nmode: subagent\nmodel: xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro\ntemperature: 0.1\ntools:\n  write: false\n  edit: false\n  bash: false\n---\n\nYou are in code review mode. Focus on:\n\n- Code quality and best practices\n- Potential bugs and edge cases\n- Performance implications\n- Security considerations\n\nProvide constructive feedback without making direct changes.\n```\n\nThe markdown file name becomes the agent name. For example, `review.md` creates a `review` agent.\n\n---\n\n## Options\n\nLet\'s look at these configuration options in detail.\n\n---\n\n### Description\n\nUse the `description` option to provide a brief description of what the agent does and when to use it.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "agent": {\n    "review": {\n      "description": "Reviews code for best practices and potential issues"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThis is a **required** config option.\n\n---\n\n### Temperature\n\nControl the randomness and creativity of the LLM\'s responses with the `temperature` config.\n\nLower values make responses more focused and deterministic, while higher values increase creativity and variability.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "agent": {\n    "plan": {\n      "temperature": 0.1\n    },\n    "creative": {\n      "temperature": 0.8\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nTemperature values typically range from 0.0 to 1.0:\n\n- **0.0-0.2**: Very focused and deterministic responses, ideal for code analysis and planning\n- **0.3-0.5**: Balanced responses with some creativity, good for general development tasks\n- **0.6-1.0**: More creative and varied responses, useful for brainstorming and exploration\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "agent": {\n    "analyze": {\n      "temperature": 0.1,\n      "prompt": "{file:./prompts/analysis.txt}"\n    },\n    "build": {\n      "temperature": 0.3\n    },\n    "brainstorm": {\n      "temperature": 0.7,\n      "prompt": "{file:./prompts/creative.txt}"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nIf no temperature is specified, MiMo Code uses model-specific defaults; typically 0 for most models, 0.55 for Qwen models.\n\n---\n\n### Max steps\n\nControl the maximum number of agentic iterations an agent can perform before being forced to respond with text only. This allows users who wish to control costs to set a limit on agentic actions.\n\nIf this is not set, the agent will continue to iterate until the model chooses to stop or the user interrupts the session.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "agent": {\n    "quick-thinker": {\n      "description": "Fast reasoning with limited iterations",\n      "prompt": "You are a quick thinker. Solve problems with minimal steps.",\n      "steps": 5\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nWhen the limit is reached, the agent receives a special system prompt instructing it to respond with a summarization of its work and recommended remaining tasks.\n\n:::caution\nThe legacy `maxSteps` field is deprecated. Use `steps` instead.\n:::\n\n---\n\n### Disable\n\nSet to `true` to disable the agent.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "agent": {\n    "review": {\n      "disable": true\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n### Prompt\n\nSpecify a custom system prompt file for this agent with the `prompt` config. The prompt file should contain instructions specific to the agent\'s purpose.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "agent": {\n    "review": {\n      "prompt": "{file:./prompts/code-review.txt}"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThis path is relative to where the config file is located. So this works for both the global MiMo Code config and the project specific config.\n\n---\n\n### Model\n\nUse the `model` config to override the model for this agent. Useful for using different models optimized for different tasks. For example, a faster model for planning, a more capable model for implementation.\n\n:::tip\nIf you don’t specify a model, primary agents use the [model globally configured](/mimocode/config-files) while subagents will use the model of the primary agent that invoked the subagent.\n:::\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "agent": {\n    "plan": {\n      "model": "xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThe model ID in your MiMo Code config uses the format `provider/model-id`. For example, with the [MiMo Platform](https://platform.xiaomimimo.com/) you would use `xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro`.\n\n---\n\n### Tools (deprecated)\n\n`tools` is **deprecated**. Prefer the agent\'s [`permission`](#permissions) field for new configs, updates and more fine-grained control.\n\nAllows you to control which tools are available in this agent. You can enable or disable specific tools by setting them to `true` or `false`. In an agent\'s `tools` config, `true` is equivalent to `{"*": "allow"}` permission and `false` is equivalent to `{"*": "deny"}` permission.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {3-6,9-12}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "tools": {\n    "write": true,\n    "bash": true\n  },\n  "agent": {\n    "plan": {\n      "tools": {\n        "write": false,\n        "bash": false\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n:::note\nThe agent-specific config overrides the global config.\n:::\n\nYou can also use wildcards in legacy `tools` entries to control multiple tools at once. For example, to disable all tools from an MCP server:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "agent": {\n    "readonly": {\n      "tools": {\n        "mymcp_*": false,\n        "write": false,\n        "edit": false\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n[Learn more about tools](/mimocode/tools).\n\n---\n\n### Permissions\n\nYou can configure permissions to manage what actions an agent can take. Currently, the permissions for the `edit`, `bash`, and `webfetch` tools can be configured to:\n\n- `"ask"` — Prompt for approval before running the tool\n- `"allow"` — Allow all operations without approval\n- `"deny"` — Disable the tool\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "permission": {\n    "edit": "deny"\n  }\n}\n```\n\nYou can override these permissions per agent.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {3-5,8-10}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "permission": {\n    "edit": "deny"\n  },\n  "agent": {\n    "build": {\n      "permission": {\n        "edit": "ask"\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nYou can also set permissions in Markdown agents.\n\n```markdown title="~/.config/mimocode/agents/review.md"\n---\ndescription: Code review without edits\nmode: subagent\npermission:\n  edit: deny\n  bash:\n    "*": ask\n    "git diff": allow\n    "git log*": allow\n    "grep *": allow\n  webfetch: deny\n---\n\nOnly analyze code and suggest changes.\n```\n\nYou can set permissions for specific bash commands.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {7}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "agent": {\n    "build": {\n      "permission": {\n        "bash": {\n          "git push": "ask",\n          "grep *": "allow"\n        }\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThis can take a glob pattern.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {7}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "agent": {\n    "build": {\n      "permission": {\n        "bash": {\n          "git *": "ask"\n        }\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nAnd you can also use the `*` wildcard to manage permissions for all commands.\nSince the last matching rule takes precedence, put the `*` wildcard first and specific rules after.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {8}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "agent": {\n    "build": {\n      "permission": {\n        "bash": {\n          "*": "ask",\n          "git status *": "allow"\n        }\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n[Learn more about permissions](/mimocode/permissions).\n\n---\n\n### Mode\n\nControl the agent\'s mode with the `mode` config. The `mode` option is used to determine how the agent can be used.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "agent": {\n    "review": {\n      "mode": "subagent"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThe `mode` option can be set to `primary`, `subagent`, or `all`. If no `mode` is specified, it defaults to `all`.\n\n---\n\n### Hidden\n\nHide a subagent from the `@` autocomplete menu with `hidden: true`. Useful for internal subagents that should only be invoked programmatically by other agents via the Task tool.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "agent": {\n    "internal-helper": {\n      "mode": "subagent",\n      "hidden": true\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThis only affects user visibility in the autocomplete menu. Hidden agents can still be invoked by the model via the Task tool if permissions allow.\n\n:::note\nOnly applies to `mode: subagent` agents.\n:::\n\n---\n\n### Task permissions\n\nControl which subagents an agent can invoke via the Task tool with `permission.task`. Uses glob patterns for flexible matching.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "agent": {\n    "orchestrator": {\n      "mode": "primary",\n      "permission": {\n        "task": {\n          "*": "deny",\n          "orchestrator-*": "allow",\n          "code-reviewer": "ask"\n        }\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nWhen set to `deny`, the subagent is removed from the Task tool description entirely, so the model won\'t attempt to invoke it.\n\n:::tip\nRules are evaluated in order, and the **last matching rule wins**. In the example above, `orchestrator-planner` matches both `*` (deny) and `orchestrator-*` (allow), but since `orchestrator-*` comes after `*`, the result is `allow`.\n:::\n\n:::tip\nUsers can always invoke any subagent directly via the `@` autocomplete menu, even if the agent\'s task permissions would deny it.\n:::\n\n---\n\n### Color\n\nCustomize the agent\'s visual appearance in the UI with the `color` option. This affects how the agent appears in the interface.\n\nUse a valid hex color (e.g., `#FF5733`) or theme color: `primary`, `secondary`, `accent`, `success`, `warning`, `error`, `info`.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "agent": {\n    "creative": {\n      "color": "#ff6b6b"\n    },\n    "code-reviewer": {\n      "color": "accent"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n### Top P\n\nControl response diversity with the `top_p` option. Alternative to temperature for controlling randomness.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "agent": {\n    "brainstorm": {\n      "top_p": 0.9\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nValues range from 0.0 to 1.0. Lower values are more focused, higher values more diverse.\n\n---\n\n### Additional\n\nAny other options you specify in your agent configuration will be **passed through directly** to the provider as model options. This allows you to use provider-specific features and parameters.\n\nFor example, with OpenAI\'s reasoning models, you can control the reasoning effort:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {6,7}\n{\n  "agent": {\n    "deep-thinker": {\n      "description": "Agent that uses high reasoning effort for complex problems",\n      "model": "openai/gpt-5",\n      "reasoningEffort": "high",\n      "textVerbosity": "low"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThese additional options are model and provider-specific. Check your provider\'s documentation for available parameters.\n\n:::tip\nRun `mimo models` to see a list of the available models.\n:::\n\n---\n\n## Create agents\n\nYou can create new agents using the following command:\n\n```bash\nmimo agent create\n```\n\nThis interactive command will:\n\n1. Ask where to save the agent; global or project-specific.\n2. Description of what the agent should do.\n3. Generate an appropriate system prompt and identifier.\n4. Let you select which tools the agent can access.\n5. Finally, create a markdown file with the agent configuration.\n\n---\n\n## Use cases\n\nHere are some common use cases for different agents.\n\n- **Build agent**: Full development work with all tools enabled\n- **Plan agent**: Analysis and planning without making changes\n- **Review agent**: Code review with read-only access plus documentation tools\n- **Debug agent**: Focused on investigation with bash and read tools enabled\n- **Docs agent**: Documentation writing with file operations but no system commands\n\n---\n\n## Examples\n\nHere are some example agents you might find useful.\n\n:::tip\nDo you have an agent you\'d like to share? [Submit a PR](https://github.com/mimocode/mimocode).\n:::\n\n---\n\n### Documentation agent\n\n```markdown title="~/.config/mimocode/agents/docs-writer.md"\n---\ndescription: Writes and maintains project documentation\nmode: subagent\ntools:\n  bash: false\n---\n\nYou are a technical writer. Create clear, comprehensive documentation.\n\nFocus on:\n\n- Clear explanations\n- Proper structure\n- Code examples\n- User-friendly language\n```\n\n---\n\n### Security auditor\n\n```markdown title="~/.config/mimocode/agents/security-auditor.md"\n---\ndescription: Performs security audits and identifies vulnerabilities\nmode: subagent\ntools:\n  write: false\n  edit: false\n---\n\nYou are a security expert. Focus on identifying potential security issues.\n\nLook for:\n\n- Input validation vulnerabilities\n- Authentication and authorization flaws\n- Data exposure risks\n- Dependency vulnerabilities\n- Configuration security issues\n```\n'},{title:"Changelog",routePath:"/mimocode/changelog",lang:"en",toc:[{text:"Changelog",id:"changelog",depth:2,charIndex:-1}],frontmatter:{title:"Changelog",description:"View MiMo Code release notes."},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode/changelog.mdx",rawContent:"## Changelog\n\nMiMo Code release notes are published in GitHub Releases.\n\n[View changelog](https://github.com/XiaomiMiMo/MiMo-Code/releases)\n"},{title:"CLI Options",routePath:"/mimocode/cli-options",lang:"en",toc:[{text:"tui",id:"tui",depth:2,charIndex:255},{text:"attach",id:"attach",depth:2,charIndex:796},{text:"github run",id:"github-run",depth:2,charIndex:1020},{text:"models",id:"models",depth:2,charIndex:1229},{text:"run",id:"run",depth:2,charIndex:1474},{text:"serve",id:"serve",depth:2,charIndex:2885},{text:"session list",id:"session-list",depth:2,charIndex:3161},{text:"stats",id:"stats",depth:2,charIndex:3409},{text:"web",id:"web",depth:2,charIndex:3811},{text:"acp",id:"acp",depth:2,charIndex:4085},{text:"uninstall",id:"uninstall",depth:2,charIndex:4299},{text:"upgrade",id:"upgrade",depth:2,charIndex:4656},{text:"Global Flags",id:"global-flags",depth:2,charIndex:4887}],frontmatter:{title:"CLI Options",description:"Reference for the flags accepted by each MiMo Code CLI command, plus global flags."},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode/cli-options.mdx",rawContent:"This page lists the flags and arguments accepted by each MiMo Code CLI command. This is stored in `~/.local/share/mimocode/auth.json`.\n\n```bash\nmimo auth login\n```\n\nWhen MiMo Code starts up it loads the providers from the credentials file. custom commands for repetitive tasks."},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode/commands.mdx",rawContent:'Custom commands let you specify a prompt you want to run when that command is executed in the TUI.\n\n```bash frame="none"\n/my-command\n```\n\nCustom commands are in addition to the built-in commands like `/init`, `/undo`, `/redo`, `/share`, `/help`. [Learn more](/mimocode/slash-commands).\n\n---\n\n## Create command files\n\nCreate markdown files in the `commands/` directory to define custom commands.\n\nCreate `.mimocode/commands/test.md`:\n\n```md title=".mimocode/commands/test.md"\n---\ndescription: Run tests with coverage\nagent: build\nmodel: xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro\n---\n\nRun the full test suite with coverage report and show any failures.\nFocus on the failing tests and suggest fixes.\n```\n\nThe frontmatter defines command properties. The content becomes the template.\n\nUse the command by typing `/` followed by the command name.\n\n```bash frame="none"\n"/test"\n```\n\n---\n\n## Configure\n\nYou can add custom commands through the MiMo Code config or by creating markdown files in the `commands/` directory.\n\n---\n\n### JSON\n\nUse the `command` option in your MiMo Code [config](/mimocode/config-files):\n\n```jsonc title="mimocode.jsonc" {4-12}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "command": {\n    // This becomes the name of the command\n    "test": {\n      // This is the prompt that will be sent to the LLM\n      "template": "Run the full test suite with coverage report and show any failures.\\nFocus on the failing tests and suggest fixes.",\n      // This is shown as the description in the TUI\n      "description": "Run tests with coverage",\n      "agent": "build",\n      "model": "xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nNow you can run this command in the TUI:\n\n```bash frame="none"\n/test\n```\n\n---\n\n### Markdown\n\nYou can also define commands using markdown files. Place them in:\n\n- Global: `~/.config/mimocode/commands/`\n- Per-project: `.mimocode/commands/`\n\n```markdown title="~/.config/mimocode/commands/test.md"\n---\ndescription: Run tests with coverage\nagent: build\nmodel: xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro\n---\n\nRun the full test suite with coverage report and show any failures.\nFocus on the failing tests and suggest fixes.\n```\n\nThe markdown file name becomes the command name. For example, `test.md` lets\nyou run:\n\n```bash frame="none"\n/test\n```\n\n---\n\n## Prompt config\n\nThe prompts for the custom commands support several special placeholders and syntax.\n\n---\n\n### Arguments\n\nPass arguments to commands using the `$ARGUMENTS` placeholder.\n\n```md title=".mimocode/commands/component.md"\n---\ndescription: Create a new component\n---\n\nCreate a new React component named $ARGUMENTS with TypeScript support.\nInclude proper typing and basic structure.\n```\n\nRun the command with arguments:\n\n```bash frame="none"\n/component Button\n```\n\nAnd `$ARGUMENTS` will be replaced with `Button`.\n\nYou can also access individual arguments using positional parameters:\n\n- `$1` - First argument\n- `$2` - Second argument\n- `$3` - Third argument\n- And so on...\n\nFor example:\n\n```md title=".mimocode/commands/create-file.md"\n---\ndescription: Create a new file with content\n---\n\nCreate a file named $1 in the directory $2\nwith the following content: $3\n```\n\nRun the command:\n\n```bash frame="none"\n/create-file config.json src "{ \\"key\\": \\"value\\" }"\n```\n\nThis replaces:\n\n- `$1` with `config.json`\n- `$2` with `src`\n- `$3` with `{ "key": "value" }`\n\n---\n\n### Shell output\n\nUse _!`command`_ to inject [bash command](/mimocode/interaction#bash-commands) output into your prompt.\n\nFor example, to create a custom command that analyzes test coverage:\n\n```md title=".mimocode/commands/analyze-coverage.md"\n---\ndescription: Analyze test coverage\n---\n\nHere are the current test results:\n!`npm test`\n\nBased on these results, suggest improvements to increase coverage.\n```\n\nOr to review recent changes:\n\n```md title=".mimocode/commands/review-changes.md"\n---\ndescription: Review recent changes\n---\n\nRecent git commits:\n!`git log --oneline -10`\n\nReview these changes and suggest any improvements.\n```\n\nCommands run in your project\'s root directory and their output becomes part of the prompt.\n\n---\n\n### File references\n\nInclude files in your command using `@` followed by the filename.\n\n```md title=".mimocode/commands/review-component.md"\n---\ndescription: Review component\n---\n\nReview the component in @src/components/Button.tsx.\nCheck for performance issues and suggest improvements.\n```\n\nThe file content gets included in the prompt automatically.\n\n---\n\n## Options\n\nLet\'s look at the configuration options in detail.\n\n---\n\n### Template\n\nThe `template` option defines the prompt that will be sent to the LLM when the command is executed.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "command": {\n    "test": {\n      "template": "Run the full test suite with coverage report and show any failures.\\nFocus on the failing tests and suggest fixes."\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThis is a **required** config option.\n\n---\n\n### Description\n\nUse the `description` option to provide a brief description of what the command does.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "command": {\n    "test": {\n      "template": "Run the full test suite with coverage.",\n      "description": "Run tests with coverage"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThis is shown as the description in the TUI when you type in the command.\n\n---\n\n### Agent\n\nUse the `agent` config to optionally specify which [agent](/mimocode/agents) should execute this command.\nIf this is a [subagent](/mimocode/agents/#subagents) the command will trigger a subagent invocation by default.\nTo disable this behavior, set `subtask` to `false`.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "command": {\n    "review": {\n      "template": "Review the current changes for bugs and style issues.",\n      "agent": "plan"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThis is an **optional** config option. If not specified, defaults to your current agent.\n\n---\n\n### Subtask\n\nUse the `subtask` boolean to force the command to trigger a [subagent](/mimocode/agents/#subagents) invocation.\nThis is useful if you want the command to not pollute your primary context and will **force** the agent to act as a subagent,\neven if `mode` is set to `primary` on the [agent](/mimocode/agents) configuration.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "command": {\n    "analyze": {\n      "template": "Analyze the codebase architecture and summarize it.",\n      "subtask": true\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThis is an **optional** config option.\n\n---\n\n### Model\n\nUse the `model` config to override the default model for this command.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "command": {\n    "analyze": {\n      "template": "Analyze the codebase architecture and summarize it.",\n      "model": "xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThis is an **optional** config option.\n'},{title:"Config Files",routePath:"/mimocode/config-files",lang:"en",toc:[{text:"Top-level fields",id:"top-level-fields",depth:2,charIndex:384},{text:"Full patterns                                      |\n| `server`             | object                | `mimo serve` / `mimo web` server settings                         |\n| `enterprise`         | object                | Enterprise-related config (`url`)                                 |\n| `username`           | string                | Custom username                                                   |\n| `logLevel`           | string                | Log level                                                         |\n| `experimental`       | object                | Experimental features, **unstable**                              |\n\n---\n\n## Full example\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "model": "xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro",\n  "small_model": "xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro",\n  "default_agent": "build",\n  "share": "manual",\n  "autoupdate": true,\n\n  "provider": {\n    "anthropic": {\n      "options": { "timeout": 600000, "setCacheKey": true }\n    }\n  },\n\n  "server": {\n    "port": 4096,\n    "hostname": "0.0.0.0",\n    "mdns": true\n  },\n\n  "permission": {\n    "*": "ask",\n    "bash": "allow",\n    "edit": "deny"\n  },\n\n  "compaction": { "auto": true, "prune": true, "tail_turns": 2 },\n  "watcher": { "ignore": ["node_modules/**", "dist/**"] },\n\n  "mcp": {},\n  "plugin": ["mimocode-helicone-session"],\n  "instructions": ["CONTRIBUTING.md"],\n\n  "disabled_providers": ["openai", "gemini"]\n}\n```\n\n> TUI-related options (`tui` / `theme` / `keybinds`) go in `~/.config/mimocode/tui.json`, not in this file.\n\n---\n\n## Field reference\n\n### `model` / `small_model`\n\nSet the default models. Format is `provider_id/model_id`. When `small_model` is unset, it falls back to the primary model or a cheaper model within the provider. See [Models & Providers](/mimocode/models-provider).\n\n### `provider`\n\nEach provider can be configured individually through `options`:\n\n| Field           | Type              | Default  | Description                                          |\n| --------------- | ----------------- | -------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |\n| `apiKey`        | string            | —        | API key                                              |\n| `baseURL`       | string            | —        | Custom endpoint URL                                  |\n| `enterpriseUrl` | string            | —        | Enterprise endpoint (e.g. GitHub Enterprise Copilot) |\n| `timeout`       | number \\| `false` | `300000` | Request timeout (ms), `false` to disable             |\n| `chunkTimeout`  | number \\| `false` | —        | Inter-chunk timeout for streaming (ms), `false` off  |\n| `setCacheKey`   | boolean           | `false`  | Always set a cache key for this provider             |\n\n`options` also passes through additional fields to the underlying SDK. For provider-specific fields (e.g. Bedrock\'s `region` / `profile` / `endpoint`), see [Provider-specific options](/mimocode/config-files).\n\n### `server`\n\nServer settings for `mimo serve` / `mimo web`:\n\n| Field        | Type            | Default          | Description                                        |\n| ------------ | --------------- | ---------------- | -------------------------------------------------- |\n| `port`       | number          | —                | Listen port                                        |\n| `hostname`   | string          | —                | Listen hostname; `0.0.0.0` when `mdns` is on and unset |\n| `mdns`       | boolean         | —                | Enable mDNS service discovery                      |\n| `mdnsDomain` | string          | `mimocode.local` | Custom mDNS domain                                 |\n| `cors`       | array\\ | —                | Allowed CORS origins                               |\n\nSee [Server](/mimocode/server).\n\n### `permission`\n\nControls whether tool actions run directly, prompt for approval, or are blocked. Can be set globally or per tool:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{ "permission": { "*": "ask", "bash": "allow", "edit": "deny" } }\n```\n\nFor the full rules, see [Permissions](/mimocode/permissions).\n\n### `compaction`\n\nAutomatic context compaction policy:\n\n| Field                    | Type    | Default | Description                              |\n| ------------------------ | ------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------- |\n| `auto`                   | boolean | `true`  | Compact automatically when context is full |\n| `prune`                  | boolean | `true`  | Remove old tool outputs to save tokens   |\n| `tail_turns`             | number  | `2`     | Number of recent turns to keep on compaction |\n| `preserve_recent_tokens` | number  | —       | Recent tokens to force-keep on compaction |\n| `reserved`               | number  | —       | Token buffer to avoid overflow during compaction |\n\n### `watcher`\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{ "watcher": { "ignore": ["node_modules/**", "dist/**"] } }\n```\n\n### `agent` / `default_agent`\n\n`agent` defines dedicated agents:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "agent": {\n    "code-reviewer": {\n      "description": "Reviews code for best practices",\n      "model": "xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro",\n      "prompt": "You are a code reviewer.",\n      "tools": { "write": false, "edit": false }\n    }\n  },\n  "default_agent": "build"\n}\n```\n\n`default_agent` must be a primary agent. An invalid value falls back to `"build"` with a warning, applied across all interfaces (TUI / `mimo run` / desktop / GitHub Action). Agents can also be defined with Markdown files; see [Agents](/mimocode/agents).\n\n### `command`\n\nCommand templates for repetitive tasks. See [Custom Commands](/mimocode/commands):\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "command": {\n    "test": {\n      "template": "Run the full test suite with coverage report.",\n      "description": "Run tests with coverage",\n      "agent": "build"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### `share`\n\n| Value        | Behavior                            |\n| ------------ | ----------------------------------- |\n| `"manual"`   | Share manually via `/share` (default) |\n| `"auto"`     | Auto-share new sessions             |\n| `"disabled"` | Fully disabled                      |\n\n### `autoupdate`\n\n| Value      | Behavior                                    |\n| ---------- | ------------------------------------------- |\n| `true`     | Auto-download new versions on startup       |\n| `false`    | Disable auto-update                         |\n| `"notify"` | Don\'t auto-update, only notify when a new version is available |\n\nAuto-update is enabled when unset. This option has no effect when installed via a package manager (e.g. Homebrew).\n\n### `formatter`\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "formatter": {\n    "prettier": { "disabled": true },\n    "custom-prettier": {\n      "command": ["npx", "prettier", "--write", "$FILE"],\n      "environment": { "NODE_ENV": "development" },\n      "extensions": [".js", ".ts", ".jsx", ".tsx"]\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nSee [Formatters](/mimocode/formatters).\n\n### `plugin`\n\nAccepts npm package names or local paths:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{ "plugin": ["mimocode-helicone-session", "./plugins/local.ts"] }\n```\n\nSee [Plugins](/mimocode/plugins).\n\n### `skills`\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{ "skills": { "paths": ["./skills/"], "urls": ["https://example.com/bundle.json"] } }\n```\n\nSee [Skills](/mimocode/skills).\n\n### `tool`\n\nControls tool invocation style:\n\n| Field                      | Type   | Description                                |\n| -------------------------- | ------ | ------------------------------------------ |\n| `invocation_style`         | string | Global invocation style (e.g. `"json"` / `"xml"`) |\n| `invocation_style_by_tool` | object | Override per tool name                     |\n\n> `tool` is a different field from the deprecated `tools`. `tools` has been merged into `permission`.\n\n### `instructions`\n\nInject additional instruction files; accepts paths and globs:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{ "instructions": ["CONTRIBUTING.md", ".cursor/rules/*.md"] }\n```\n\nSee [Rules](/mimocode/rules).\n\n### `disabled_providers` / `enabled_providers`\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "disabled_providers": ["openai", "gemini"],\n  "enabled_providers": ["anthropic", "openai"]\n}\n```\n\n- A disabled provider won\'t load even if its environment variable is set or a key is configured via `/connect`, and its models won\'t appear in the selection list.\n- When `enabled_providers` is set, only providers in the list are enabled; all others are ignored.\n- `disabled_providers` takes precedence over `enabled_providers`.\n\n### Other fields\n\n- **`mcp`** — MCP servers, see [MCP](/mimocode/mcp-servers)\n- **`lsp`** — LSP integration, see [LSP](/mimocode/lsp)\n- **`enterprise`** — `{ "url": "https://enterprise.example.com" }`\n- **`checkpoint`** — checkpoint thresholds and quotas (`thresholds` / `reserved` / `push_caps`); refer to the schema\n- **`experimental`** — experimental features that may change or be removed without notice\n\n---\n\n## Variable substitution\n\nConfig values can reference environment variables and file contents:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "model": "{env:MIMOCODE_MODEL}",\n  "provider": {\n    "anthropic": {\n      "options": {\n        "apiKey": "{env:ANTHROPIC_API_KEY}",\n        "baseURL": "{file:~/.secrets/anthropic-endpoint}"\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n- `{env:VAR}` — replaced with an empty string when unset\n- `{file:path}` — relative to the config file directory, or an absolute path starting with `/` or `~`\n\n:::note\nPlaceholders inside `//` single-line comments in JSONC are not substituted.\n:::\n\n---\n\n## Schema\n\nConfig files follow [`mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json`](https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json). Add `"$schema"` at the top of the file to enable editor completion and validation:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{ "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json" }\n```\n'},{title:"Config Overrides",routePath:"/mimocode/config-overrides",lang:"en",toc:[{text:"The three roles of environment variables",id:"the-three-roles-of-environment-variables",depth:2,charIndex:399},{text:"Precedence for ordinary runtime parameters",id:"precedence-for-ordinary-runtime-parameters",depth:2,charIndex:1355},{text:"Config files",id:"config-files",depth:2,charIndex:2554},{text:"Provider credentials",id:"provider-credentials",depth:2,charIndex:4505},{text:"Process-level environment variables",id:"process-level-environment-variables",depth:2,charIndex:5491},{text:"Command-line options",id:"command-line-options",depth:2,charIndex:6322},{text:"Typical scenarios",id:"typical-scenarios",depth:2,charIndex:10125},{text:'Deprecated "remote endpoint" name',id:"deprecated-remote-endpoint-name",depth:2,charIndex:10523}],frontmatter:{title:"Config Overrides",description:"Where MiMo Code runtime parameters come from, their precedence, and typical scenarios."},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode/config-overrides.mdx",rawContent:'MiMo Code runtime parameters come from three places: the user config file, command-line options, and process-level environment variables. Each serves a different purpose, and **environment variables are not a general fallback for config fields**—they need to be understood by category. The CLI executable is named `mimo`.\n\n---\n\n## The three roles of environment variables\n\nEnvironment variables serve three distinct, non-overlapping roles in MiMo Code. For the full variable table, see [Environment Variables](/mimocode/env-vars).\n\n1. **Resource location and injection** — `MIMOCODE_HOME` sets the profile root, `MIMOCODE_CONFIG` / `MIMOCODE_CONFIG_DIR` / `MIMOCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT` point directly at config sources, and `MIMOCODE_PERMISSION` inlines a `permission` block.\n2. **Runtime switches** — the `MIMOCODE_DISABLE_*` and `MIMOCODE_ENABLE_*` families control whether a capability is enabled, with the semantics of "an extra switch on top of config." For example, `MIMOCODE_DISABLE_AUTOUPDATE` turns off auto-update even if the config says `autoupdate: true`.\n3. **Auth and endpoints** — `MIMOCODE_AUTH_CONTENT` / `MIMOCODE_CONSOLE_TOKEN` / `MIMOCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` and friends inject credentials for CI and server mode.\n\n---\n\n## Precedence for ordinary runtime parameters\n\nThe resolution order for non-environment-variable parameters is: **command-line options > config file**. But a few points first.\n\n- Most CLI flags (`--model` / `--agent` / `--variant` / `--prompt` / `--continue`, etc.) **apply only to the current prompt\'s run** and are not written back to `mimocode.json`; the next session still reads the config file values.\n- Network flags (`--port` / `--hostname` / `--mdns` / `--mdns-domain` / `--cors`) override `config.server.*` only when **actually passed** on the command line. Among them, `--cors` **appends** to the config CORS list rather than replacing it.\n- A few environment variables explicitly override config fields: e.g. `MIMOCODE_AUTO_SHARE=true` is equivalent to `share: "auto"`, and `MIMOCODE_DISABLE_AUTOCOMPACT=true` is equivalent to turning off `compaction.auto`. This "env var overrides config field" case is the exception—see the individual field notes.\n\n:::note\n**There is no `--config` or `--config-file` flag.** To specify a config file, use `MIMOCODE_CONFIG` or `MIMOCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT`. To switch the entire profile root, use `MIMOCODE_HOME`.\n:::\n\n---\n\n## Config files\n\nThe location of config files is controlled by `MIMOCODE_HOME`; when unset, the global config defaults to `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/mimocode/` (equivalent to `~/.config/mimocode/` on macOS and Linux).\n\n`MIMOCODE_HOME` must be an **absolute path** (a `~/` prefix is not accepted), otherwise startup errors out. Once set, the following four base directories all move under that path:\n\n```\n$MIMOCODE_HOME/\n├── config/    ← global config files\n├── data/      ← auth.json, session database\n├── state/\n└── cache/\n```\n\nFile name rules:\n\n- The **global directory** accepts `config.json` / `mimocode.json` / `mimocode.jsonc`, merged in that order (later overrides earlier).\n- The **project directory** only accepts `mimocode.json` / `mimocode.jsonc`, searched from the current directory up to the worktree root, merged layer by layer with **parent first, current last**. Other names (e.g. `opencode.json`) are not loaded.\n- `.mimocode/` and `MIMOCODE_CONFIG_DIR` likewise use `mimocode.json(c)`.\n\nMerge rules:\n\n- The general merge is a **deep merge**, recursing per key on objects.\n- Arrays are replaced wholesale by default; **`instructions` is the exception**, deduplicated and concatenated.\n- **`plugin` / `mcp` entries with the same name are overridden by the later writer** (consistent with deep merge). Exception: when the Claude Code compatibility layer injects `~/.claude.json`\'s `mcpServers` into `mcp`, it **skips** any entry whose name already exists in `mcp`, to avoid overriding the user\'s explicit config.\n- The `tools` field is translated into derived `permission` entries, but **a user\'s explicit `permission` takes precedence**.\n- `theme` / `keybinds` / `tui` in `mimocode.json` are **silently dropped** with a prompt to migrate them to `~/.config/mimocode/tui.json`.\n- macOS **MDM managed preferences** (`/Library/Managed Preferences/.../ai.opencode.managed.plist`) override wholesale on top of all user-layer config (including `MIMOCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT`), and ordinary users cannot bypass them.\n\nFor field definitions, see [Config Files](/mimocode/config-files).\n\n---\n\n## Provider credentials\n\nCredentials (API keys, OAuth tokens, etc.) are **not stored in `mimocode.json`**; they live in a separate auth file:\n\n- Read by default from `$XDG_DATA_HOME/mimocode/auth.json` with `0o600` permissions; it moves along with `MIMOCODE_HOME` when that is set.\n- `MIMOCODE_AUTH_CONTENT` takes a full JSON string as inline credentials, for CI scenarios.\n- In the default **mimo-only mode** (`MIMOCODE_MIMO_ONLY=true`), provider environment variables such as `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` / `OPENAI_API_KEY` are **not** read from the shell as a fallback. **Only `MIMOCODE_MIMO_ONLY=false` restores** this fallback; setting `MIMOCODE_DISABLE_PROVIDER_ENV=false` alone has no effect (the flag is always true while mimo-only is true).\n- To keep credentials in `mimocode.json` without exposing them in version control, use the `{env:VAR}` or `{file:path}` placeholders (see [Config Files \xb7 Variable substitution](/mimocode/config-files#variable-substitution)).\n\n---\n\n## Process-level environment variables\n\nThe practical effects of process-level environment variables cluster in the following directions, matching the "three roles" above:\n\n- **Resource root and config injection** — `MIMOCODE_HOME` / `MIMOCODE_CONFIG` / `MIMOCODE_CONFIG_DIR` / `MIMOCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT` / `MIMOCODE_PERMISSION`.\n- **Runtime switches** — `MIMOCODE_DISABLE_*` / `MIMOCODE_ENABLE_*` / `MIMOCODE_PURE`.\n- **Auth and endpoints** — `MIMOCODE_AUTH_CONTENT` / `MIMOCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` / `MIMOCODE_SERVER_USERNAME` / `MIMOCODE_CONSOLE_TOKEN` / `MIMOCODE_WORKSPACE_ID`.\n- **Diagnostics and internal** — `MIMOCODE_SHOW_TTFD` / `MIMOCODE_AUTO_HEAP_SNAPSHOT`, etc., not recommended for regular use.\n\nFor the full variables, defaults, and semantics, see [Environment Variables](/mimocode/env-vars).\n\n---\n\n## Command-line options\n\nThe table below lists the most common global options, grouped by subcommand, that either interact with config or change the behavior of the current run. For the full list, see [Command Line](/mimocode/cli-options).\n\n| Option                            | Subcommands                          | Effect                                                                  |\n| --------------------------------- | ------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `-m, --model `    | root / `run`                         | Model used for this session                                             |\n| `--agent `                  | root / `run`                         | Agent used for this session                                             |\n| `--variant `                | `run`                                | Reasoning effort, free-form string, common values `high` / `max` / `minimal` |\n| `-c, --continue`                  | root / `run` / `attach`              | Continue the last session                                               |\n| `-s, --session `              | root / `run` / `attach`              | Specify a session ID                                                    |\n| `--fork`                          | root / `run` / `attach`              | Fork from the given session (use with `--continue` or `--session`)      |\n| `--prompt `                 | root                                 | Initial TUI prompt                                                      |\n| `-f, --file `               | `run`                                | Attach a file, repeatable                                               |\n| `--share`                         | `run`                                | Enable sharing for this session                                         |\n| `--dangerously-skip-permissions`  | `run`                                | Auto-approve permission requests that aren\'t explicitly denied          |\n| `--format `        | `run`                                | Event-stream output format for `run`                                    |\n| `--port `                      | root / `serve` / `web` / `acp` / `run` | Listen port; overrides `config.server.port` when explicitly passed    |\n| `--hostname `               | root / `serve` / `web` / `acp`       | Listen hostname; overrides `config.server.hostname` when explicitly passed |\n| `--mdns`                          | root / `serve` / `web` / `acp`       | Enable mDNS service discovery                                           |\n| `--mdns-domain `          | root / `serve` / `web` / `acp`       | mDNS domain                                                             |\n| `--cors `                 | root / `serve` / `web` / `acp`       | Allowed CORS origin; **appends** to `config.server.cors`, not replace   |\n| `--pure`                          | root                                 | Skip all external plugins (equivalent to `MIMOCODE_PURE=1`)             |\n| `--print-logs`                    | root                                 | Output logs to stderr                                                   |\n| `--log-level `             | root                                 | `DEBUG` / `INFO` / `WARN` / `ERROR`                                     |\n| `-p, --password `            | `run` / `attach`                     | Basic-auth password for a remote server (equivalent to `MIMOCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD`) |\n| `--dir `                    | `run` / `attach`                     | Working directory; in attach mode refers to the remote path             |\n\nThe `attach` subcommand requires a **positional argument** `` pointing at the remote server: `mimo attach  [flags...]`.\n\nMutual exclusion and dependencies:\n\n- `--continue` and `--session` **cannot both** specify a new session source; `--fork` must be used with one of them.\n- When `--mdns` is enabled and **neither `--hostname` is passed nor `server.hostname` is set in config**, hostname defaults to `0.0.0.0`; if hostname is specified anywhere, that value is used.\n- `--share` is subject to `MIMOCODE_DISABLE_SHARE`; this flag has no effect when that is `true`.\n- `--dangerously-skip-permissions` is only valid under the `run` subcommand; inside the TUI it is enabled via a slash command.\n\n---\n\n## Typical scenarios\n\n**Switch the profile root for isolated testing.**\n\n```sh\nMIMOCODE_HOME=/tmp/mimo-test mimo\n```\n\n**Inline config and credentials in CI without relying on disk files.**\n\n```sh\nMIMOCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT=\'{"model":"xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro","share":"disabled"}\' \\\nMIMOCODE_AUTH_CONTENT=\'{"anthropic":{"apiKey":"sk-..."}}\' \\\n  mimo run "Generate release notes"\n```\n\n**Skip all permission prompts for this run (`run` only).**\n\n```sh\nmimo run --dangerously-skip-permissions "Format all TypeScript files"\n```\n\n**Temporarily switch model for this run without changing the default.**\n\n```sh\nmimo run -m xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro "Summarize the diff"\n```\n\n**Enable basic auth and switch ports for `serve`.**\n\n```sh\nMIMOCODE_SERVER_USERNAME=alice MIMOCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD=s3cret \\\n  mimo serve --port 4096\n```\n\n---\n\n## Deprecated "remote endpoint" name\n\nHistorical docs wrote the remote well-known endpoint as `.well-known/mimocode`. **The source actually requests `.well-known/opencode`**; use the latter when setting your own organization defaults.\n'},{title:"Custom Tools",routePath:"/mimocode/custom-tools",lang:"en",toc:[{text:"Creating a tool",id:"creating-a-tool",depth:2,charIndex:239},{text:"Location",id:"location",depth:3,charIndex:538},{text:"Structure",id:"structure",depth:3,charIndex:795},{text:"Multiple tools per file",id:"multiple-tools-per-file",depth:4,charIndex:1066},{text:"Name collisions with built-in tools",id:"name-collisions-with-built-in-tools",depth:4,charIndex:1360},{text:"Arguments",id:"arguments",depth:3,charIndex:1830},{text:"Context",id:"context",depth:3,charIndex:2061},{text:"Examples",id:"examples",depth:2,charIndex:2312},{text:"Write a tool in Python",id:"write-a-tool-in-python",depth:3,charIndex:2324}],frontmatter:{title:"Custom Tools",description:"Create tools the LLM can call in MiMo Code."},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode/custom-tools.mdx",rawContent:'Custom tools are functions you create that the LLM can call during conversations. They work alongside mimocode\'s [built-in tools](/mimocode/tools) like `read`, `write`, and `bash`.\n\n---\n\n## Creating a tool\n\nTools are defined as **TypeScript** or **JavaScript** files. However, the tool definition can invoke scripts written in **any language** — TypeScript or JavaScript is only used for the tool definition itself.\n\n---\n\n### Location\n\nThey can be defined:\n\n- Locally by placing them in the `.mimocode/tools/` directory of your project.\n- Or globally, by placing them in `~/.config/mimocode/tools/`.\n\n---\n\n### Structure\n\nThe easiest way to create tools is using the `tool()` helper which provides type-safety and validation.\n\n```ts title=".mimocode/tools/database.ts" {1}\nimport { tool } from "@mimocode/cli/plugin"\n\nexport default tool({\n  description: "Query the project database",\n  args: {\n    query: tool.schema.string().describe("SQL query to execute"),\n  },\n  async execute(args) {\n    // Your database logic here\n    return `Executed query: ${args.query}`\n  },\n})\n```\n\nThe **filename** becomes the **tool name**. The above creates a `database` tool.\n\n---\n\n#### Multiple tools per file\n\nYou can also export multiple tools from a single file. Each export becomes **a separate tool** with the name **`_`**:\n\n```ts title=".mimocode/tools/math.ts"\nimport { tool } from "@mimocode/cli/plugin"\n\nexport const add = tool({\n  description: "Add two numbers",\n  args: {\n    a: tool.schema.number().describe("First number"),\n    b: tool.schema.number().describe("Second number"),\n  },\n  async execute(args) {\n    return args.a + args.b\n  },\n})\n\nexport const multiply = tool({\n  description: "Multiply two numbers",\n  args: {\n    a: tool.schema.number().describe("First number"),\n    b: tool.schema.number().describe("Second number"),\n  },\n  async execute(args) {\n    return args.a * args.b\n  },\n})\n```\n\nThis creates two tools: `math_add` and `math_multiply`.\n\n---\n\n#### Name collisions with built-in tools\n\nCustom tools are keyed by tool name. If a custom tool uses the same name as a built-in tool, the custom tool takes precedence.\n\nFor example, this file replaces the built-in `bash` tool:\n\n```ts title=".mimocode/tools/bash.ts"\nimport { tool } from "@mimocode/cli/plugin"\n\nexport default tool({\n  description: "Restricted bash wrapper",\n  args: {\n    command: tool.schema.string(),\n  },\n  async execute(args) {\n    return `blocked: ${args.command}`\n  },\n})\n```\n\n:::note\nPrefer unique names unless you intentionally want to replace a built-in tool. If you want to disable a built in tool but not override it, use [permissions](/mimocode/permissions).\n:::\n\n---\n\n### Arguments\n\nYou can use `tool.schema`, which is just [Zod](https://zod.dev), to define argument types.\n\n```ts "tool.schema"\nargs: {\n  query: tool.schema.string().describe("SQL query to execute")\n}\n```\n\nYou can also import [Zod](https://zod.dev) directly and return a plain object:\n\n```ts {6}\nimport { z } from "zod"\n\nexport default {\n  description: "Tool description",\n  args: {\n    param: z.string().describe("Parameter description"),\n  },\n  async execute(args, context) {\n    // Tool implementation\n    return "result"\n  },\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n### Context\n\nTools receive context about the current session:\n\n```ts title=".mimocode/tools/project.ts" {8}\nimport { tool } from "@mimocode/cli/plugin"\n\nexport default tool({\n  description: "Get project information",\n  args: {},\n  async execute(args, context) {\n    // Access context information\n    const { agent, sessionID, messageID, directory, worktree } = context\n    return `Agent: ${agent}, Session: ${sessionID}, Message: ${messageID}, Directory: ${directory}, Worktree: ${worktree}`\n  },\n})\n```\n\nUse `context.directory` for the session working directory.\nUse `context.worktree` for the git worktree root.\n\n---\n\n## Examples\n\n### Write a tool in Python\n\nYou can write your tools in any language you want. Here\'s an example that adds two numbers using Python.\n\nFirst, create the tool as a Python script:\n\n```python title=".mimocode/tools/add.py"\nimport sys\n\na = int(sys.argv[1])\nb = int(sys.argv[2])\nprint(a + b)\n```\n\nThen create the tool definition that invokes it:\n\n```ts title=".mimocode/tools/python-add.ts" {10}\nimport { tool } from "@mimocode/cli/plugin"\nimport path from "path"\n\nexport default tool({\n  description: "Add two numbers using Python",\n  args: {\n    a: tool.schema.number().describe("First number"),\n    b: tool.schema.number().describe("Second number"),\n  },\n  async execute(args, context) {\n    const script = path.join(context.worktree, ".mimocode/tools/add.py")\n    const result = await Bun.$`python3 ${script} ${args.a} ${args.b}`.text()\n    return result.trim()\n  },\n})\n```\n\nHere we are using the [`Bun.$`](https://bun.com/docs/runtime/shell) utility to run the Python script.\n'},{title:"Environment Variables",routePath:"/mimocode/env-vars",lang:"en",toc:[{text:"Resource location",id:"resource-location",depth:2,charIndex:621},{text:"Runtime switches",id:"runtime-switches",depth:2,charIndex:1690},{text:"Compatibility and skipping",id:"compatibility-and-skipping",depth:2,charIndex:3996},{text:"Auth and endpoints",id:"auth-and-endpoints",depth:2,charIndex:5734},{text:"Experimental",id:"experimental",depth:2,charIndex:6380},{text:"Diagnostics and internal",id:"diagnostics-and-internal",depth:2,charIndex:8128},{text:"Typical scenarios",id:"typical-scenarios",depth:2,charIndex:9335}],frontmatter:{title:"Environment Variables",description:"A categorized reference of all environment variables MiMo Code supports, with typical scenarios."},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode/env-vars.mdx",rawContent:'In MiMo Code, environment variables are **not a general fallback for the config file**. They serve four distinct roles: locating resources, runtime switches, compatibility and skipping, and auth and endpoints. Config fields (such as `model` / `permission` / `server.port`) are **not** read automatically from same-named environment variables; they must be injected explicitly via `MIMOCODE_CONFIG` or `MIMOCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT`.\n\nBoolean variables follow a consistent rule: `true` / `1` means on, `false` / `0` means off; any other value or unset uses the default.\n\n---\n\n## Resource location\n\n| Variable                  | Type   | Default | Effect                                                                                          |\n| ------------------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `MIMOCODE_HOME`           | string | —       | Single profile root (holds `config/`, `data/`, `state/`, `cache/`), **overrides all XDG base dirs**, must be an absolute path |\n| `MIMOCODE_CONFIG`         | string | —       | Custom config file path                                                                         |\n| `MIMOCODE_CONFIG_DIR`     | string | —       | Custom config directory (same structure as `.mimocode/`)                                        |\n| `MIMOCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT` | string | —       | Inline JSON config content                                                                      |\n| `MIMOCODE_TUI_CONFIG`     | string | —       | Custom TUI config file path                                                                     |\n| `MIMOCODE_PERMISSION`     | string | —       | Inline JSON permission config                                                                   |\n| `MIMOCODE_DB`             | string | —       | Override the database file path                                                                 |\n| questions.\n\n---\n\n## Initialize\n\nNavigate to a project that you want to work on.\n\n```bash\ncd /path/to/project\n```\n\nAnd run MiMo Code.\n\n```bash\nmimo\n```\n\nNext, initialize MiMo Code for the project by running the following command.\n\n```bash frame="none"\n/init\n```\n\nThis will get MiMo Code to analyze your project and create an `AGENTS.md` file in\nthe project root.\n\n:::tip\nYou should commit your project\'s `AGENTS.md` file to Git.\n:::\n\nThis helps MiMo Code understand the project structure and the coding patterns\nused.\n\n---\n\n## Usage\n\nYou are now ready to use MiMo Code to work on your project. Feel free to ask it\nanything!\n\nIf you are new to using an AI coding agent, here are some examples that might\nhelp.\n\n---\n\n### Ask questions\n\nYou can ask MiMo Code to explain the codebase to you.\n\n:::tip\nUse the `@` key to fuzzy search for files in the project.\n:::\n\n```txt frame="none" "@packages/functions/src/api/index.ts"\nHow is authentication handled in @packages/functions/src/api/index.ts\n```\n\nThis is helpful if there\'s a part of the codebase that you didn\'t work on.\n\n---\n\n### Add features\n\nYou can ask MiMo Code to add new features to your project. Though we first recommend asking it to create a plan.\n\n1. **Create a plan**\n\n   MiMo Code has a _Plan mode_ that disables its ability to make changes and\n   instead suggest _how_ it\'ll implement the feature.\n\n   Switch to it using the **Tab** key. You\'ll see an indicator for this in the lower right corner.\n\n   ```bash frame="none" title="Switch to Plan mode"\n   \n   ```\n\n   Now let\'s describe what we want it to do.\n\n   ```txt frame="none"\n   When a user deletes a note, we\'d like to flag it as deleted in the database.\n   Then create a screen that shows all the recently deleted notes.\n   From this screen, the user can undelete a note or permanently delete it.\n   ```\n\n   You want to give MiMo Code enough details to understand what you want. It helps\n   to talk to it like you are talking to a junior developer on your team.\n\n   :::tip\n   Give MiMo Code plenty of context and examples to help it understand what you\n   want.\n   :::\n\n2. **Iterate on the plan**\n\n   Once it gives you a plan, you can give it feedback or add more details.\n\n   ```txt frame="none"\n   We\'d like to design this new screen using a design I\'ve used before.\n   [Image #1] Take a look at this image and use it as a reference.\n   ```\n\n   :::tip\n   Drag and drop images into the terminal to add them to the prompt.\n   :::\n\n   MiMo Code can scan any images you give it and add them to the prompt. You can\n   do this by dragging and dropping an image into the terminal.\n\n3. **Build the feature**\n\n   Once you feel comfortable with the plan, switch back to _Build mode_ by\n   hitting the **Tab** key again.\n\n   ```bash frame="none"\n   \n   ```\n\n   And asking it to make the changes.\n\n   ```bash frame="none"\n   Sounds good! Go ahead and make the changes.\n   ```\n\n---\n\n### Make changes\n\nFor more straightforward changes, you can ask MiMo Code to directly build it\nwithout having to review the plan first.\n\n```txt frame="none" "@packages/functions/src/settings.ts" "@packages/functions/src/notes.ts"\nWe need to add authentication to the /settings route. Take a look at how this is\nhandled in the /notes route in @packages/functions/src/notes.ts and implement\nthe same logic in @packages/functions/src/settings.ts\n```\n\nYou want to make sure you provide a good amount of detail so MiMo Code makes the right\nchanges.\n\n---\n\n### Undo changes\n\nLet\'s say you ask MiMo Code to make some changes.\n\n```txt frame="none" "@packages/functions/src/api/index.ts"\nCan you refactor the function in @packages/functions/src/api/index.ts?\n```\n\nBut you realize that it is not what you wanted. You **can undo** the changes\nusing the `/undo` command.\n\n```bash frame="none"\n/undo\n```\n\nMiMo Code will now revert the changes you made and show your original message\nagain.\n\n```txt frame="none" "@packages/functions/src/api/index.ts"\nCan you refactor the function in @packages/functions/src/api/index.ts?\n```\n\nFrom here you can tweak the prompt and ask MiMo Code to try again.\n\n:::tip\nYou can run `/undo` multiple times to undo multiple changes.\n:::\n\nOr you **can redo** the changes using the `/redo` command.\n\n```bash frame="none"\n/redo\n```\n\n---\n\n## Customize\n\nAnd that\'s it! You are now a pro at using MiMo Code.\n\nTo make it your own, we recommend [picking a theme](/mimocode/themes), [customizing the keybinds](/mimocode/keybinds), [configuring code formatters](/mimocode/formatters), [creating custom commands](/mimocode/commands), or playing around with the [MiMo Code config](/mimocode/config-files).\n'},{title:"Formatters",routePath:"/mimocode/formatters",lang:"en",toc:[{text:"Built-in",id:"built-in",depth:2,charIndex:276},{text:"How it works",id:"how-it-works",depth:2,charIndex:3816},{text:"Configure",id:"configure",depth:2,charIndex:4235},{text:"Disabling formatters",id:"disabling-formatters",depth:3,charIndex:4817},{text:"Custom formatters",id:"custom-formatters",depth:3,charIndex:5044}],frontmatter:{title:"Formatters",description:"MiMo Code uses language specific formatters."},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode/formatters.mdx",rawContent:'MiMo Code automatically formats files after they are written or edited using language-specific formatters. This ensures that the code that is generated follows the code styles of your project.\n\n---\n\n## Built-in\n\nMiMo Code comes with several built-in formatters for popular languages and frameworks. | .rs                                                                                                      | `cargo fmt` command available                                                                         |\n| clang-format         | .c, .cpp, .h, .hpp, .ino, and [more](https://clang.llvm.org/docs/ClangFormat.html)                       | `.clang-format` config file                                                                                          | `gleam` command available                                                                             |\n|                                                                    |\n| ktlint               | .kt, .kts                                                                                                | `ktlint` command available                                                                            |\n| mix                  | .ex, .exs, .eex, .heex, .leex, .neex, .sface                                                                         | `nixfmt` command available                                                                            |\n| ocamlformat          | .ml, .mli                                                                                                | `ocamlformat` command available and `.ocamlformat` config file              | `laravel/pint` dependency in `composer.json`                                                          |\n| prettier             | .js, .jsx, .ts, .tsx, .html, .css, .md, .json, .yaml,                                                                         |\n| shfmt                | .sh, .bash                                                                                               | `shfmt` command available                                                                             |\n| standardrb           | .rb, .rake, .gemspec, .ru                |\n| zig                  | .zig, .zon                                                                                               | `zig` command available                                                                               |\n\nSo if your project has `prettier` in your `package.json`, MiMo Code will automatically use it.\n\n---\n\n## How it works\n\nWhen MiMo Code writes or edits a file, it:\n\n1. Checks the file extension against all enabled formatters.\n2. Runs the appropriate formatter command on the file.\n3. Applies the formatting changes automatically.\n\nThis process happens in the background, ensuring your code styles are maintained without any manual steps.\n\n---\n\n## Configure\n\nYou can customize formatters through the `formatter` section in your MiMo Code config.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "formatter": {}\n}\n```\n\nEach formatter configuration supports the following:\n\n| Property      | Type     | Description                                             |\n| ------------- | -------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `disabled`    | boolean  | Set this to `true` to disable the formatter             |\n| `command`     | string[] | The command to run for formatting                       |\n| `environment` | object   | Environment variables to set when running the formatter |\n| `extensions`  | string[] | File extensions this formatter should handle            |\n\nLet\'s look at some examples.\n\n---\n\n### Disabling formatters\n\nTo disable **all** formatters globally, set `formatter` to `false`:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {3}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "formatter": false\n}\n```\n\nTo disable a **specific** formatter, set `disabled` to `true`:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {5}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "formatter": {\n    "prettier": {\n      "disabled": true\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n### Custom formatters\n\nYou can override the built-in formatters or add new ones by specifying the command, environment variables, and file extensions:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {4-14}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "formatter": {\n    "prettier": {\n      "command": ["npx", "prettier", "--write", "$FILE"],\n      "environment": {\n        "NODE_ENV": "development"\n      },\n      "extensions": [".js", ".ts", ".jsx", ".tsx"]\n    },\n    "custom-markdown-formatter": {\n      "command": ["deno", "fmt", "$FILE"],\n      "extensions": [".md"]\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThe **`$FILE` placeholder** in the command will be replaced with the path to the file being formatted.\n'},{title:"Install & A modern terminal emulator like:\n   - [WezTerm](https://wezterm.org), cross-platform\n   - [Alacritty](https://alacritty.org), cross-platform\n   - [Ghostty](https://ghostty.org), Linux and macOS\n   - [Kitty](https://sw.kovidgoyal.net/kitty/), Linux and macOS\n\n2. This page covers how to enter messages, reference files, run commands, and steer the model while you work in the TUI.\n\nRunning MiMo Code starts the TUI for the current directory.\n\n```bash\nmimo\n```\n\nOr you can start it for a specific working directory.\n\n```bash\nmimo /path/to/project\n```\n\nOnce you\'re in the TUI, you can prompt it with a message.\n\n```text\nGive me a quick summary of the codebase.\n```\n\nTo run MiMo Code programmatically from the command line instead, see [Command Line](/mimocode/cli-options).\n\n---\n\n## Input basics\n\nType your message in the input box at the bottom and press `enter` to send.\n\n- **New line:** press `shift+enter`, `ctrl+j`, `ctrl+return`, or `alt+return` to insert a line break without sending.\n- **In-line editing:** the input box supports common cursor and deletion shortcuts, e.g. `ctrl+a` / `ctrl+e` to jump to the start/end of the line, `ctrl+w` to delete the previous word, `ctrl+u` to delete to the start of the line. See the full list in [Keybinds](/mimocode/keybinds#desktop-prompt-shortcuts).\n- **Exit:** press `ctrl+c` or `ctrl+d`, or use the `/exit` command.\n\n:::tip\nMost actions use the `ctrl+x` leader key: press `ctrl+x` first, then the action key. For example, start a new session with `ctrl+x` then `n`. [Learn more](/mimocode/keybinds).\n:::\n\n---\n\n## File references\n\nYou can reference files in your messages using `@`. This does a fuzzy file search in the current working directory.\n\n```text "@packages/functions/src/api/index.ts"\nHow is auth handled in @packages/functions/src/api/index.ts?\n```\n\nThe content of the file is added to the conversation automatically.\n\n---\n\n## Image input\n\nMiMo Code can scan images you provide and add them to your prompt. Just **drag and drop the image into the terminal window**.\n\n```txt frame="none"\n[Image #1] Take a look at this image and use it as a reference.\n```\n\nImage support depends on whether the selected model supports multimodal input.\n\n---\n\n## Bash commands\n\nStart a message with `!` to run a shell command.\n\n```bash frame="none"\n!ls -la\n```\n\nThe output of the command is added to the conversation as a tool result.\n\n---\n\n## Slash commands\n\nType `/` followed by a command name to quickly execute actions. For example:\n\n```bash frame="none"\n/help\n```\n\nMost commands also have a keybind using `ctrl+x` as the leader key. For the full list of built-in commands, see [Slash Commands](/mimocode/slash-commands); for keybinds, see [Keybinds](/mimocode/keybinds).\n\n---\n\n## Mode switching and interrupts\n\nMiMo Code provides two built-in working modes, **Build** and **Plan**. Build mode can read and write files and run commands directly; Plan mode only outputs an action plan without modifying files, which suits complex or high-risk tasks.\n\n- **Switch modes:** press `tab` during a session to toggle between Build and Plan.\n- **Interrupt a response:** while the model is streaming output or calling tools, press `esc` to interrupt the current turn.\n\nFor more on configuring and customizing modes, see [Modes](/mimocode/modes).\n\n---\n\n## Configure\n\nYou can customize TUI behavior through `tui.json` (or `tui.jsonc`).\n\n```json title="tui.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/tui.json",\n  "theme": "mimocode",\n  "keybinds": {\n    "leader": "ctrl+x"\n  },\n  "scroll_speed": 3,\n  "scroll_acceleration": {\n    "enabled": true\n  },\n  "diff_style": "auto",\n  "mouse": true\n}\n```\n\nThis is separate from `mimocode.json`, which configures server/runtime behavior.\n\n### Options\n\n- `theme` - Sets your UI theme. [Learn more](/mimocode/themes).\n- `keybinds` - Customizes keyboard shortcuts. [Learn more](/mimocode/keybinds).\n- `scroll_acceleration.enabled` - Enable macOS-style scroll acceleration for smooth, natural scrolling. When enabled, scroll speed increases with rapid scrolling gestures and stays precise for slower movements. **This setting takes precedence over `scroll_speed` and overrides it when enabled.**\n- `scroll_speed` - Controls how fast the TUI scrolls when using scroll commands (minimum: `0.001`, supports decimal values). Defaults to `3`. **Note: This is ignored if `scroll_acceleration.enabled` is set to `true`.**\n- `diff_style` - Controls diff rendering. `"auto"` adapts to terminal width, `"stacked"` always shows a single-column layout.\n- `mouse` - Enable or disable mouse capture in the TUI (default: `true`). When disabled, the terminal\'s native mouse selection/scrolling behavior is preserved.\n\nUse `MIMOCODE_TUI_CONFIG` to load a custom TUI config path.\n'},{title:"Keybinds",routePath:"/mimocode/keybinds",lang:"en",toc:[{text:"Leader key",id:"leader-key",depth:2,charIndex:159},{text:"Disable This avoids conflicts in your terminal.\n\nBy default, `ctrl+x` is the leader key and most actions require you to first press the leader key and then the shortcut. For example, to start a new session you first press `ctrl+x` and then press `n`.\n\nYou don\'t need to use a leader key for your keybinds but we recommend doing so.\n\nSome navigation keybinds intentionally do not use the leader key by default. For subagent sessions, the defaults are `session_child_first` = `\\down`, `session_child_cycle` = `right`, `session_child_cycle_reverse` = `left`, and `session_parent` = `up`.\n\n---\n\n## Disable keybind\n\nYou can disable a keybind by adding the key to `tui.json` with a value of "none".\n\n```json title="tui.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/tui.json",\n  "keybinds": {\n    "session_compact": "none"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Desktop prompt shortcuts\n\nThe MiMo Code desktop app prompt input supports common Readline/Emacs-style shortcuts for editing text. These are built-in and currently not configurable via `mimocode.json`.\n\n| Shortcut | Action                                   |\n| -------- | ---------------------------------------- |\n| `ctrl+a` | Move to start of current line            |\n| `ctrl+e` | Move to end of current line              |\n| `ctrl+b` | Move cursor back one character           |\n| `ctrl+f` | Move cursor forward one character        |\n| `alt+b`  | Move cursor back one word                |\n| `alt+f`  | Move cursor forward one word             |\n| `ctrl+d` | Delete character under cursor            |\n| `ctrl+k` | Kill to end of line                      |\n| `ctrl+u` | Kill to start of line                    |\n| `ctrl+w` | Kill previous word                       |\n| `alt+d`  | Kill next word                           |\n| `ctrl+t` | Transpose characters                     |\n| `ctrl+g` | Cancel popovers / abort running response |\n\n---\n\n## Shift+Enter\n\nSome terminals don\'t send modifier keys with Enter by default.
