Checks the file extension against all enabled LSP servers.\n2. Starts the appropriate LSP server if not already running.\n\n---\n\n## Configure\n\nYou can customize LSP servers through the `lsp` section in your mimocode config.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "lsp": {}\n}\n```\n\nEach LSP server supports the following:\n\n| Property         | Type     | Description                                       |\n| ---------------- | -------- | ------------------------------------------------- |\n| `disabled`       | boolean  | Set this to `true` to disable the LSP server      |\n| `command`        | string[] | The command to start the LSP server               |\n| `extensions`     | string[] | File extensions this LSP server should handle     |\n| `env`            | object   | Environment variables to set when starting server |\n| `initialization` | object   | Initialization options to send to the LSP server  |\n\nLet\'s look at some examples.\n\n---\n\n### Environment variables\n\nUse the `env` property to set environment variables when starting the LSP server:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {6-8}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "lsp": {\n    "rust": {\n      "command": ["rust-analyzer"],\n      "env": {\n        "RUST_LOG": "debug"\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n### Initialization options\n\nUse the `initialization` property to pass initialization options to the LSP server. These are server-specific settings sent during the LSP `initialize` request:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {6-10}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "lsp": {\n    "typescript": {\n      "command": ["typescript-language-server", "--stdio"],\n      "initialization": {\n        "preferences": {\n          "importModuleSpecifierPreference": "relative"\n        }\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n:::note\nInitialization options vary by LSP server. Check your LSP server\'s documentation for available options.\n:::\n\n---\n\n### Disabling LSP servers\n\nTo disable **all** LSP servers globally, set `lsp` to `false`:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {3}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "lsp": false\n}\n```\n\nTo disable a **specific** LSP server, set `disabled` to `true`:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {5}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "lsp": {\n    "typescript": {\n      "disabled": true\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n### Custom LSP servers\n\nYou can add custom LSP servers by specifying the command and file extensions:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {4-7}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "lsp": {\n    "custom-lsp": {\n      "command": ["custom-lsp-server", "--stdio"],\n      "extensions": [".custom"]\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Additional Information\n\n### PHP Intelephense\n\nPHP Intelephense offers premium features through a license key. You can provide a license key by placing (only) the key in a text file at:\n\n- On macOS/Linux: `$HOME/intelephense/license.txt`\n- On Windows: `%USERPROFILE%/intelephense/license.txt`\n\nThe file should contain only the license key with no additional content.\n'},{title:"MCP servers",routePath:"/mimocode/mcp-servers",lang:"en",toc:[{text:"Caveats",id:"caveats",depth:4,charIndex:302},{text:"Enable",id:"enable",depth:2,charIndex:774},{text:"Overriding remote defaults",id:"overriding-remote-defaults",depth:3,charIndex:1180},{text:"Local",id:"local",depth:2,charIndex:1685},{text:"Options",id:"options",depth:4,charIndex:2130},{text:"Remote",id:"remote",depth:2,charIndex:2819},{text:"Options",id:"options-1",depth:4,charIndex:3073},{text:"OAuth",id:"oauth",depth:2,charIndex:3730},{text:"Automatic",id:"automatic",depth:3,charIndex:4131},{text:"Pre-registered",id:"pre-registered",depth:3,charIndex:4523},{text:"Authenticating",id:"authenticating",depth:3,charIndex:4711},{text:"Disabling OAuth",id:"disabling-oauth",depth:4,charIndex:5163},{text:"OAuth Options",id:"oauth-options",depth:4,charIndex:5385},{text:"Debugging",id:"debugging",depth:4,charIndex:5922},{text:"Manage",id:"manage",depth:2,charIndex:6219},{text:"Global",id:"global",depth:3,charIndex:6458},{text:"Per agent",id:"per-agent",depth:3,charIndex:6739},{text:"Glob patterns",id:"glob-patterns",depth:4,charIndex:7055},{text:"Examples",id:"examples",depth:2,charIndex:7508},{text:"Sentry",id:"sentry",depth:3,charIndex:7709},{text:"Context7",id:"context7",depth:3,charIndex:8154},{text:"Grep by Vercel",id:"grep-by-vercel",depth:3,charIndex:8617}],frontmatter:{title:"MCP servers",description:"Add local and remote MCP tools."},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode/mcp-servers.mdx",rawContent:'You can add external tools to MiMo Code using the _Model Context Protocol_, or MCP. MiMo Code supports both local and remote servers.\n\nOnce added, MCP tools are automatically available to the LLM alongside built-in tools.\n\n---\n\n#### Caveats\n\nWhen you use an MCP server, it adds to the context. This can quickly add up if you have a lot of tools. So we recommend being careful with which MCP servers you use.\n\n:::tip\nMCP servers add to your context, so you want to be careful with which ones you enable.\n:::\n\nCertain MCP servers, like the GitHub MCP server, tend to add a lot of tokens and can easily exceed the context limit.\n\n---\n\n## Enable\n\nYou can define MCP servers in your [MiMo Code Config](/mimocode/config-files) under `mcp`. Add each MCP with a unique name. You can refer to that MCP by name when prompting the LLM.\n\n```jsonc title="mimocode.jsonc" {6}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "mcp": {\n    "name-of-mcp-server": {\n      // ...\n      "enabled": true,\n    },\n    "name-of-other-mcp-server": {\n      // ...\n    },\n  },\n}\n```\n\nYou can also disable a server by setting `enabled` to `false`. This is useful if you want to temporarily disable a server without removing it from your config.\n\n---\n\n### Overriding remote defaults\n\nOrganizations can provide default MCP servers via their `.well-known/mimocode` endpoint. These servers may be disabled by default, allowing users to opt-in to the ones they need.\n\nTo enable a specific server from your organization\'s remote config, add it to your local config with `enabled: true`:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "mcp": {\n    "jira": {\n      "type": "remote",\n      "url": "https://jira.example.com/mcp",\n      "enabled": true\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nYour local config values override the remote defaults. See [config precedence](/mimocode/config-files) for more details.\n\n---\n\n## Local\n\nAdd local MCP servers using `type` to `"local"` within the MCP object.\n\n```jsonc title="mimocode.jsonc" {15}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "mcp": {\n    "my-local-mcp-server": {\n      "type": "local",\n      // Or ["bun", "x", "my-mcp-command"]\n      "command": ["npx", "-y", "my-mcp-command"],\n      "enabled": true,\n      "environment": {\n        "MY_ENV_VAR": "my_env_var_value",\n      },\n    },\n  },\n}\n```\n\nThe command is how the local MCP server is started. You can also pass in a list of environment variables as well.\n\nFor example, here\'s how you can add the test [`@modelcontextprotocol/server-everything`](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@modelcontextprotocol/server-everything) MCP server.\n\n```jsonc title="mimocode.jsonc"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "mcp": {\n    "mcp_everything": {\n      "type": "local",\n      "command": ["npx", "-y", "@modelcontextprotocol/server-everything"],\n    },\n  },\n}\n```\n\nAnd to use it I can add `use the mcp_everything tool` to my prompts.\n\n```txt "mcp_everything"\nuse the mcp_everything tool to add the number 3 and 4\n```\n\n---\n\n#### Options\n\nHere are all the options for configuring a local MCP server.\n\n| Option        | Type    | Required | Description                                                                         |\n| ------------- | ------- | -------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `type`        | String  | Y        | Type of MCP server connection, must be `"local"`.                                   |\n| `command`     | Array   | Y        | Command and arguments to run the MCP server.                                        |\n| `environment` | Object  |          | Environment variables to set when running the server.                               |\n| `enabled`     | Boolean |          | Enable or disable the MCP server on startup.                                        |\n| `timeout`     | Number  |          | Timeout in ms for fetching tools from the MCP server. Defaults to 5000 (5 seconds). |\n\n---\n\n## Remote\n\nAdd remote MCP servers by setting `type` to `"remote"`.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "mcp": {\n    "my-remote-mcp": {\n      "type": "remote",\n      "url": "https://my-mcp-server.com",\n      "enabled": true,\n      "headers": {\n        "Authorization": "Bearer MY_API_KEY"\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThe `url` is the URL of the remote MCP server and with the `headers` option you can pass in a list of headers.\n\n---\n\n#### Options\n\n| Option    | Type    | Required | Description                                                                         |\n| --------- | ------- | -------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `type`    | String  | Y        | Type of MCP server connection, must be `"remote"`.                                  |\n| `url`     | String  | Y        | URL of the remote MCP server.                                                       |\n| `enabled` | Boolean |          | Enable or disable the MCP server on startup.                                        |\n| `headers` | Object  |          | Headers to send with the request.                                                   |\n| `oauth`   | Object  |          | OAuth authentication configuration. See [OAuth](#oauth) section below.              |\n| `timeout` | Number  |          | Timeout in ms for fetching tools from the MCP server. Defaults to 5000 (5 seconds). |\n\n---\n\n## OAuth\n\nMiMo Code automatically handles OAuth authentication for remote MCP servers. When a server requires authentication, MiMo Code will:\n\n1. Detect the 401 response and initiate the OAuth flow\n2. Use **Dynamic Client Registration (RFC 7591)** if supported by the server\n3. Store tokens securely for future requests\n\n---\n\n### Automatic\n\nFor most OAuth-enabled MCP servers, no special configuration is needed. Just configure the remote server:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "mcp": {\n    "my-oauth-server": {\n      "type": "remote",\n      "url": "https://mcp.example.com/mcp"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nIf the server requires authentication, MiMo Code will prompt you to authenticate when you first try to use it. If not, you can [manually trigger the flow](#authenticating) with `mimo mcp auth `.\n\n---\n\n### Pre-registered\n\nIf you have client credentials from the MCP server provider, you can configure them:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {7-11}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "mcp": {\n    "my-oauth-server": {\n      "type": "remote",\n      "url": "https://mcp.example.com/mcp",\n      "oauth": {\n        "clientId": "{env:MY_MCP_CLIENT_ID}",\n        "clientSecret": "{env:MY_MCP_CLIENT_SECRET}",\n        "scope": "tools:read tools:execute"\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n### Authenticating\n\nYou can manually trigger authentication or manage credentials.\n\nAuthenticate with a specific MCP server:\n\n```bash\nmimo mcp auth my-oauth-server\n```\n\nList all MCP servers and their auth status:\n\n```bash\nmimo mcp list\n```\n\nRemove stored credentials:\n\n```bash\nmimo mcp logout my-oauth-server\n```\n\nThe `mcp auth` command will open your browser for authorization. After you authorize, MiMo Code will store the tokens securely in `~/.local/share/mimocode/mcp-auth.json`.\n\n---\n\n#### Disabling OAuth\n\nIf you want to disable automatic OAuth for a server (e.g., for servers that use API keys instead), set `oauth` to `false`:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {7}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "mcp": {\n    "my-api-key-server": {\n      "type": "remote",\n      "url": "https://mcp.example.com/mcp",\n      "oauth": false,\n      "headers": {\n        "Authorization": "Bearer {env:MY_API_KEY}"\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n#### OAuth Options\n\n| Option         | Type            | Description                                                                      |\n| -------------- | --------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `oauth`        | Object \\| false | OAuth config object, or `false` to disable OAuth auto-detection.                 |\n| `clientId`     | String          | OAuth client ID. If not provided, dynamic client registration will be attempted. |\n| `clientSecret` | String          | OAuth client secret, if required by the authorization server.                    |\n| `scope`        | String          | OAuth scopes to request during authorization.                                    |\n\n#### Debugging\n\nIf a remote MCP server is failing to authenticate, you can diagnose issues with:\n\n```bash\n# View auth status for all OAuth-capable servers\nmimo mcp auth list\n\n# Debug connection and OAuth flow for a specific server\nmimo mcp debug my-oauth-server\n```\n\nThe `mcp debug` command shows the current auth status, tests HTTP connectivity, and attempts the OAuth discovery flow.\n\n---\n\n## Manage\n\nYour MCPs are available as tools in MiMo Code, alongside built-in tools. So you can manage them through the MiMo Code config like any other tool.\n\n---\n\n### Global\n\nThis means that you can enable or disable them globally.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {14}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "mcp": {\n    "my-mcp-foo": {\n      "type": "local",\n      "command": ["bun", "x", "my-mcp-command-foo"]\n    },\n    "my-mcp-bar": {\n      "type": "local",\n      "command": ["bun", "x", "my-mcp-command-bar"]\n    }\n  },\n  "tools": {\n    "my-mcp-foo": false\n  }\n}\n```\n\nWe can also use a glob pattern to disable all matching MCPs.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {14}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "mcp": {\n    "my-mcp-foo": {\n      "type": "local",\n      "command": ["bun", "x", "my-mcp-command-foo"]\n    },\n    "my-mcp-bar": {\n      "type": "local",\n      "command": ["bun", "x", "my-mcp-command-bar"]\n    }\n  },\n  "tools": {\n    "my-mcp*": false\n  }\n}\n```\n\nHere we are using the glob pattern `my-mcp*` to disable all MCPs.\n\n---\n\n### Per agent\n\nIf you have a large number of MCP servers you may want to only enable them per agent and disable them globally. To do this:\n\n1. Disable it as a tool globally.\n2. In your [agent config](/mimocode/agents#tools), enable the MCP server as a tool.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {11, 14-18}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "mcp": {\n    "my-mcp": {\n      "type": "local",\n      "command": ["bun", "x", "my-mcp-command"],\n      "enabled": true\n    }\n  },\n  "tools": {\n    "my-mcp*": false\n  },\n  "agent": {\n    "my-agent": {\n      "tools": {\n        "my-mcp*": true\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n#### Glob patterns\n\nThe glob pattern uses simple regex globbing patterns:\n\n- `*` matches zero or more of any character (e.g., `"my-mcp*"` matches `my-mcp_search`, `my-mcp_list`, etc.)\n- `?` matches exactly one character\n- All other characters match literally\n\n:::note\nMCP server tools are registered with server name as prefix, so to disable all tools for a server simply use:\n\n```\n"mymcpservername_*": false\n```\n\n:::\n\n---\n\n## Examples\n\nBelow are examples of some common MCP servers. You can submit a PR if you want to document other servers.\n\n---\n\n### Sentry\n\nAdd the [Sentry MCP server](https://mcp.sentry.dev) to interact with your Sentry projects and issues.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {4-8}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "mcp": {\n    "sentry": {\n      "type": "remote",\n      "url": "https://mcp.sentry.dev/mcp",\n      "oauth": {}\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nAfter adding the configuration, authenticate with Sentry:\n\n```bash\nmimo mcp auth sentry\n```\n\nThis will open a browser window to complete the OAuth flow and connect MiMo Code to your Sentry account.\n\nOnce authenticated, you can use Sentry tools in your prompts to query issues, projects, and error data.\n\n```txt "use sentry"\nShow me the latest unresolved issues in my project. use sentry\n```\n\n---\n\n### Context7\n\nAdd the [Context7 MCP server](https://github.com/upstash/context7) to search through docs.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {4-7}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "mcp": {\n    "context7": {\n      "type": "remote",\n      "url": "https://mcp.context7.com/mcp"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nIf you have signed up for a free account, you can use your API key and get higher rate-limits.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {7-9}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "mcp": {\n    "context7": {\n      "type": "remote",\n      "url": "https://mcp.context7.com/mcp",\n      "headers": {\n        "CONTEXT7_API_KEY": "{env:CONTEXT7_API_KEY}"\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nHere we are assuming that you have the `CONTEXT7_API_KEY` environment variable set.\n\nAdd `use context7` to your prompts to use Context7 MCP server.\n\n```txt "use context7"\nConfigure a Cloudflare Worker script to cache JSON API responses for five minutes. use context7\n```\n\nAlternatively, you can add something like this to your [AGENTS.md](/mimocode/rules/).\n\n```md title="AGENTS.md"\nWhen you need to search docs, use `context7` tools.\n```\n\n---\n\n### Grep by Vercel\n\nAdd the [Grep by Vercel](https://grep.app) MCP server to search through code snippets on GitHub.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {4-7}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "mcp": {\n    "gh_grep": {\n      "type": "remote",\n      "url": "https://mcp.grep.app"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nSince we named our MCP server `gh_grep`, you can add `use the gh_grep tool` to your prompts to get the agent to use it.\n\n```txt "use the gh_grep tool"\nWhat\'s the right way to set a custom domain in an SST Astro component? use the gh_grep tool\n```\n\nAlternatively, you can add something like this to your [AGENTS.md](/mimocode/rules/).\n\n```md title="AGENTS.md"\nIf you are unsure how to do something, use `gh_grep` to search code examples from GitHub.\n```\n'},{title:"Models",routePath:"/mimocode/models-provider",lang:"en",toc:[{text:"Connect a providers** and it supports running local models.\n\n---\n\n## Connect a provider\n\nWith MiMo Code you can use any LLM provider by configuring their API keys.\n\nIf you are new to using LLM providers, we recommend using [MiMo Token Plan](https://platform.xiaomimimo.com/token-plan). It\'s a curated list of models that have been tested and verified by the MiMo Code team.\n\n1. Run the `/connect` command in the TUI, select Xiaomi MiMo, and head to [platform.xiaomimimo.com](https://platform.xiaomimimo.com/).\n\n   ```txt\n   /connect\n   ```\n\n2. [Learn more](/mimocode/models-provider).\n\n---\n\n## Providers\n\nMost popular providers are preloaded by default. If you\'ve added the credentials for a provider through the `/connect` command, they\'ll be available when you start MiMo Code.\n\nLearn more about [providers](/mimocode/models-provider).\n\n---\n\n## Select a model\n\nOnce you\'ve configured your provider you can select the model you want by typing in:\n\n```bash frame="none"\n/models\n```\n\n---\n\n## MiMo Platform (recommended)\n\nThe fastest way to get started is the [MiMo Platform](https://platform.xiaomimimo.com/). It is OpenAI-compatible and authenticates with the `api-key` HTTP header. Drop the following into your `mimocode.jsonc`:\n\n```jsonc title="mimocode.jsonc"\n{\n  "provider": {\n    "mimo": {\n      "npm": "@ai-sdk/openai-compatible",\n      "name": "MiMo",\n      "options": {\n        "baseURL": "https://api.xiaomimimo.com/v1",\n        "headers": { "api-key": "{env:MIMO_API_KEY}" }\n      },\n      "models": {\n        "mimo-v2.5-pro": { "name": "MiMo V2.5 Pro" }\n      }\n    }\n  },\n  "model": "mimo/mimo-v2.5-pro"\n}\n```\n\nThe flagship model is `mimo-v2.5-pro`. Reasoning output is returned as `reasoning_content`. An Anthropic-compatible endpoint is also available at `https://api.xiaomimimo.com/anthropic`.\n\n:::note\nIn the example above, `mimo` is the key you chose for your own `provider` entry, so the model is referenced as `mimo/mimo-v2.5-pro`. The **built-in** Xiaomi provider has the id `xiaomi`, so if you connect via `/connect` instead of defining a custom provider, reference the model as `xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro`. Run `mimo models` to see the exact IDs available to you.\n:::\n\n---\n\n## Loading models\n\nWhen MiMo Code starts up, it checks for models in the following priority order:\n\n1. The `--model` or `-m` command line flag. The format is the same as in the config file: `provider_id/model_id`.\n\n2. The model list in the MiMo Code config.\n\n   ```json title="mimocode.json"\n   {\n     "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n     "model": "xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro"\n   }\n   ```\n\n   The format here is `provider/model`.\n\n3. The last used model.\n\n4. The first model using an internal priority.\n'},{title:"Models",routePath:"/mimocode/models",lang:"en",toc:[{text:"Set a default",id:"set-a-default",depth:2,charIndex:-1},{text:"Configure models",id:"configure-models",depth:2,charIndex:475},{text:"Variants",id:"variants",depth:2,charIndex:1120},{text:"Built-in variants",id:"built-in-variants",depth:3,charIndex:1272},{text:"Custom variants",id:"custom-variants",depth:3,charIndex:1846},{text:"Cycle variants",id:"cycle-variants",depth:3,charIndex:1920}],frontmatter:{title:"Models",description:"Set a default model, configure model options, and use variants."},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode/models.mdx",rawContent:'## Set a default\n\nTo set one of these as the default model, you can set the `model` key in your\nMiMo Code config.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json" {3}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "model": "xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro"\n}\n```\n\nHere the full ID is `provider_id/model_id`. For example, if you\'re using the [MiMo Platform](https://platform.xiaomimimo.com/), you would use `xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro`.\n\nIf you\'ve configured a [custom provider](/mimocode/models-provider), the `provider_id` is key from the `provider` part of your config, and the `model_id` is the key from `provider.models`.\n\n---\n\n## Configure models\n\nYou can globally configure a model\'s options through the config.\n\n```jsonc title="mimocode.jsonc" {5-12}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "provider": {\n    "mimo": {\n      "models": {\n        "mimo-v2.5-pro": {\n          "options": {\n            "reasoningEffort": "high",\n            "textVerbosity": "low",\n            "reasoningSummary": "auto",\n          },\n        },\n      },\n    },\n  },\n}\n```\n\nHere we\'re configuring global settings for the `mimo-v2.5-pro` model when accessed via the `mimo` provider.\nThe built-in provider and model names can be found on [Models.dev](https://models.dev).\n\nYou can also configure these options for any agents that you are using. The agent config overrides any global options here. [Learn more](/mimocode/agents/#additional).\n\nYou can also define custom variants that extend built-in ones. Variants let you configure different settings for the same model without creating duplicate entries:\n\n```jsonc title="mimocode.jsonc" {6-21}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "provider": {\n    "mimo": {\n      "models": {\n        "mimo-v2.5-pro": {\n          "variants": {\n            "high": {\n              "reasoningEffort": "high",\n              "textVerbosity": "low",\n              "reasoningSummary": "auto",\n            },\n            "low": {\n              "reasoningEffort": "low",\n              "textVerbosity": "low",\n              "reasoningSummary": "auto",\n            },\n          },\n        },\n      },\n    },\n  },\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Variants\n\nMany models support multiple variants with different configurations. MiMo Code ships with built-in default variants for popular providers.\n\n### Built-in variants\n\nMiMo Code ships with default variants for many providers:\n\n**Anthropic**:\n\n- `high` - High thinking budget (default)\n- `max` - Maximum thinking budget\n\n**OpenAI**:\n\nVaries by model but roughly:\n\n- `none` - No reasoning\n- `minimal` - Minimal reasoning effort\n- `low` - Low reasoning effort\n- `medium` - Medium reasoning effort\n- `high` - High reasoning effort\n- `xhigh` - Extra high reasoning effort\n\n**Google**:\n\n- `low` - Lower effort/token budget\n- `high` - Higher effort/token budget\n\n:::tip\nThis list is not comprehensive. Many other providers have built-in defaults too.\n:::\n\n### Custom variants\n\nYou can override existing variants or add your own:\n\n```jsonc title="mimocode.jsonc" {7-18}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "provider": {\n    "mimo": {\n      "models": {\n        "mimo-v2.5-pro": {\n          "variants": {\n            "thinking": {\n              "reasoningEffort": "high",\n              "textVerbosity": "low",\n            },\n            "fast": {\n              "disabled": true,\n            },\n          },\n        },\n      },\n    },\n  },\n}\n```\n\n### Cycle variants\n\nUse the keybind `variant_cycle` to quickly switch between variants. [Learn more](/mimocode/keybinds).\n'},{title:"Modes",routePath:"/mimocode/modes",lang:"en",toc:[{text:"Built-in",id:"built-in",depth:2,charIndex:-1},{text:"Build",id:"build",depth:3,charIndex:768},{text:"Plan",id:"plan",depth:3,charIndex:1024},{text:"Compose",id:"compose",depth:3,charIndex:1604},{text:"Overview",id:"overview",depth:4,charIndex:2166},{text:"Enable Compose mode",id:"enable-compose-mode",depth:4,charIndex:2586},{text:"Built-in skills",id:"built-in-skills",depth:4,charIndex:3025},{text:"Switching",id:"switching",depth:2,charIndex:4048}],frontmatter:{title:"Modes",description:"Different modes for different use cases."},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode/modes.mdx",rawContent:"## Built-in\n\nMiMo Code comes with three built-in modes (primary agents), each with a distinct role:\n\n- **`build`** — default primary agent with full tool access for general development work.\n- **`plan`** — restricted primary agent for read-only analysis and planning.\n- **`compose`** — primary agent that orchestrates work through built-in skills.\n\nThere are also **`general`** / **`explore`** — subagents invoked by primary agents for delegated tasks.\n\n`compose` is a verb form, matching the `build`/`plan`/`explore` naming convention. It does not replace `build`; it complements it by adding a workflow-aware mode where the model is encouraged to think in terms of named, reusable skills rather than ad-hoc procedures.\n\n---\n\n### Build\n\nBuild is the **default** mode with all tools enabled. This is the standard mode for development work where you need full access to file operations and system commands.\n\n---\n\n### Plan\n\nA restricted mode designed for planning and analysis. In plan mode, the following tools are disabled by default:\n\n- `write` - Cannot create new files\n- `edit` - Cannot modify existing files, except for files located at `.mimocode/plans/*.md` to detail the plan itself\n- `patch` - Cannot apply patches\n- `bash` - Cannot execute shell commands\n\nThis mode is useful when you want the AI to analyze code, suggest changes, or create plans without making any actual modifications to your codebase.\n\n---\n\n### Compose\n\nCompose is a primary agent in MiMo Code that orchestrates task execution through a curated set of built-in skills. Instead of relying on a single monolithic prompt, Compose consults a library of 13 focused skills—covering testing, debugging, planning, collaboration, and meta-development—and selects the right skill for each step of your workflow.\n\n:::tip\nSwitch to the Compose agent when you want a workflow-driven loop: brainstorm a feature, write a plan, execute it with TDD, verify before completion, and request a code review—all guided by skills.\n:::\n\n#### Overview\n\nCompose was inspired by the open-source [superpowers](https://github.com/obra/superpowers) workflow and ported into MiMo Code as a first-class primary agent. Like `build` and `plan`, Compose handles your main conversation and can be selected with the **Tab** key. What sets it apart is the bundled skill library: every skill ships with the binary, is loaded at runtime under the `compose:` namespace, and is available only when the Compose agent is active.\n\n#### Enable Compose mode\n\nCompose is registered as a built-in primary agent under the name `compose`. To activate it:\n\n1. Press **Tab** (or your configured `switch_agent` keybind) to cycle through primary agents until **Compose** is selected.\n2. Or invoke it with `@compose` in a message.\n\nNo additional configuration is required. The skill bundle is extracted to `{data}/compose/{version}/` on first use and kept up to date across MiMo Code releases.\n\n#### Built-in skills\n\nCompose ships with 13 skills, grouped by category. They are referenced from the model side via the `compose:` namespace.\n\n##### Testing\n\n| Skill | Purpose |\n| --- | --- |\n| `compose:tdd` | Test-driven development workflow |\n\n##### Debugging\n\n| Skill | Purpose |\n| --- | --- |\n| `compose:debug` | Systematic debugging methodology |\n| `compose:verify` | Verification before completion |\n\n##### Collaboration\n\n| Skill | Purpose |\n| --- | --- |\n| `compose:brainstorm` | Brainstorming with the user |\n| `compose:plan` | Writing implementation plans |\n| `compose:execute` | Executing an approved plan |\n| `compose:parallel` | Dispatching parallel agents |\n| `compose:review` | Requesting a code review |\n| `compose:feedback` | Receiving and applying review feedback |\n| `compose:worktree` | Working in a git worktree |\n| `compose:merge` | Finishing a development branch |\n| `compose:subagent` | Subagent-driven development |\n\n##### Meta\n\n| Skill | Purpose |\n| --- | --- |\n| `compose:new-skill` | Authoring a new skill |\n\n---\n\n## Switching\n\nYou can switch between modes during a session using the _Tab_ key. Or your configured `switch_mode` keybind.\n\nSee also: [Formatters](/mimocode/formatters) for information about code formatting configuration.\n\n"},{title:"Network",routePath:"/mimocode/network",lang:"en",toc:[{text:"Proxy",id:"proxy",depth:2,charIndex:200},{text:"Authenticate",id:"authenticate",depth:3,charIndex:547},{text:"Custom certificates",id:"custom-certificates",depth:2,charIndex:961}],frontmatter:{title:"Network",description:"Configure proxies and custom certificates."},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode/network.mdx",rawContent:"MiMo Code supports standard proxy environment variables and custom certificates for enterprise network environments.\n\n---\n\n## Proxy\n\nMiMo Code respects standard proxy environment variables.\n\n```bash\n# HTTPS proxy (recommended)\nexport HTTPS_PROXY=https://proxy.example.com:8080\n\n# HTTP proxy (if HTTPS not available)\nexport HTTP_PROXY=http://proxy.example.com:8080\n\n# Bypass proxy for local server (required)\nexport NO_PROXY=localhost,127.0.0.1\n```\n\n:::caution\nThe TUI communicates with a local HTTP server. You must bypass the proxy for this connection to prevent routing loops.\n:::\n\nYou can configure the server's port and hostname using [CLI flags](/mimocode/cli-options).\n\n---\n\n### Authenticate\n\nIf your proxy requires basic authentication, include credentials in the URL.\n\n```bash\nexport HTTPS_PROXY=http://username:password@proxy.example.com:8080\n```\n\n:::caution\nAvoid hardcoding passwords. Use environment variables or secure credential storage.\n:::\n\nFor proxies requiring advanced authentication like NTLM or Kerberos, consider using an LLM Gateway that supports your authentication method.\n\n---\n\n## Custom certificates\n\nIf your enterprise uses custom CAs for HTTPS connections, configure MiMo Code to trust them.\n\n```bash\nexport NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS=/path/to/ca-cert.pem\n```\n\nThis works for both proxy connections and direct API access.\n"},{title:"Permissions",routePath:"/mimocode/permissions",lang:"en",toc:[{text:"Actions",id:"actions",depth:2,charIndex:373},{text:"Configuration",id:"configuration",depth:2,charIndex:603},{text:"Granular Rules (Object Syntax)",id:"granular-rules-object-syntax",depth:2,charIndex:822},{text:"Wildcards",id:"wildcards",depth:3,charIndex:1122},{text:"Home Directory Expansion",id:"home-directory-expansion",depth:3,charIndex:1305},{text:"External Directories",id:"external-directories",depth:3,charIndex:1596},{text:"Available Permissions",id:"available-permissions",depth:2,charIndex:2658},{text:"Defaults",id:"defaults",depth:2,charIndex:3681},{text:"What “Ask” Does",id:"what-ask-does",depth:2,charIndex:4005},{text:"Agents",id:"agents",depth:2,charIndex:4520}],frontmatter:{title:"Permissions",description:"Control which actions require approval to run."},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode/permissions.mdx",rawContent:'MiMo Code uses the `permission` config to decide whether a given action should run automatically, prompt you, or be blocked.\n\nAs of `v1.1.1`, the legacy `tools` boolean config is deprecated and has been merged into `permission`. The old `tools` config is still supported for backwards compatibility.\n\n---\n\n## Actions\n\nEach permission rule resolves to one of:\n\n- `"allow"` — run without approval\n- `"ask"` — prompt for approval\n- `"deny"` — block the action\n\n---\n\n## Configuration\n\nYou can set permissions globally (with `*`), and override specific tools.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "permission": {\n    "*": "ask",\n    "bash": "allow",\n    "edit": "deny"\n  }\n}\n```\n\nYou can also set all permissions at once:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "permission": "allow"\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Granular Rules (Object Syntax)\n\nFor most permissions, you can use an object to apply different actions based on the tool input.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "permission": {\n    "bash": {\n      "*": "ask",\n      "git *": "allow",\n      "npm *": "allow",\n      "rm *": "deny",\n      "grep *": "allow"\n    },\n    "edit": {\n      "*": "deny",\n      "packages/web/src/content/docs/*.mdx": "allow"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nRules are evaluated by pattern match, with the **last matching rule winning**. A common pattern is to put the catch-all `"*"` rule first, and more specific rules after it.\n\n### Wildcards\n\nPermission patterns use simple wildcard matching:\n\n- `*` matches zero or more of any character\n- `?` matches exactly one character\n- All other characters match literally\n\n### Home Directory Expansion\n\nYou can use `~` or `$HOME` at the start of a pattern to reference your home directory. This is particularly useful for [`external_directory`](#external-directories) rules.\n\n- `~/projects/*` -> `/Users/username/projects/*`\n- `$HOME/projects/*` -> `/Users/username/projects/*`\n- `~` -> `/Users/username`\n\n### External Directories\n\nUse `external_directory` to allow tool calls that touch paths outside the working directory where MiMo Code was started. This applies to any tool that takes a path as input (for example `read`, `edit`, `glob`, `grep`, and many `bash` commands).\n\nHome expansion (like `~/...`) only affects how a pattern is written. It does not make an external path part of the current workspace, so paths outside the working directory must still be allowed via `external_directory`.\n\nFor example, this allows access to everything under `~/projects/personal/`:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "permission": {\n    "external_directory": {\n      "~/projects/personal/**": "allow"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nAny directory allowed here inherits the same defaults as the current workspace. Since [`read` defaults to `allow`](#defaults), reads are also allowed for entries under `external_directory` unless overridden. Add explicit rules when a tool should be restricted in these paths, such as blocking edits while keeping reads:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "permission": {\n    "external_directory": {\n      "~/projects/personal/**": "allow"\n    },\n    "edit": {\n      "~/projects/personal/**": "deny"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nKeep the list focused on trusted paths, and layer extra allow or deny rules as needed for other tools (for example `bash`).\n\n---\n\n## Available Permissions\n\nMiMo Code permissions are keyed by tool name, plus a couple of safety guards:\n\n- `read` — reading a file (matches the file path)\n- `edit` — all file modifications (covers `edit`, `write`, `patch`, `multiedit`)\n- `glob` — file globbing (matches the glob pattern)\n- `grep` — content search (matches the regex pattern)\n- `bash` — running shell commands (matches parsed commands like `git status --porcelain`)\n- `task` — launching subagents (matches the subagent type)\n- `skill` — loading a skill (matches the skill name)\n- `lsp` — running LSP queries (currently non-granular)\n- `question` — asking the user questions during execution\n- `webfetch` — fetching a URL (matches the URL)\n- `websearch`, `codesearch` — web/code search (matches the query)\n- `external_directory` — triggered when a tool touches paths outside the project working directory\n- `doom_loop` — triggered when the same tool call repeats 3 times with identical input\n\n---\n\n## Defaults\n\nIf you don’t specify anything, MiMo Code starts from permissive defaults:\n\n- Most permissions default to `"allow"`.\n- `doom_loop` and `external_directory` default to `"ask"`.\n- `read` is `"allow"`, but `.env` files are denied by default:\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "permission": {\n    "read": {\n      "*": "allow",\n      "*.env": "deny",\n      "*.env.*": "deny",\n      "*.env.example": "allow"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## What “Ask” Does\n\nWhen MiMo Code prompts for approval, the UI offers three outcomes:\n\n- `once` — approve just this request\n- `always` — approve future requests matching the suggested patterns (for the rest of the current MiMo Code session)\n- `reject` — deny the request\n\nThe set of patterns that `always` would approve is provided by the tool (for example, bash approvals typically whitelist a safe command prefix like `git status*`).\n\n---\n\n## Agents\n\nYou can override permissions per agent. Agent permissions are merged with the global config, and agent rules take precedence. [Learn more](/mimocode/agents#permissions) about agent permissions.\n\n:::note\nRefer to the [Granular Rules (Object Syntax)](#granular-rules-object-syntax) section above for more detailed pattern matching examples.\n:::\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "permission": {\n    "bash": {\n      "*": "ask",\n      "git *": "allow",\n      "git commit *": "deny",\n      "git push *": "deny",\n      "grep *": "allow"\n    }\n  },\n  "agent": {\n    "build": {\n      "permission": {\n        "bash": {\n          "*": "ask",\n          "git *": "allow",\n          "git commit *": "ask",\n          "git push *": "deny",\n          "grep *": "allow"\n        }\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nYou can also configure agent permissions in Markdown:\n\n```markdown title="~/.config/mimocode/agents/review.md"\n---\ndescription: Code review without edits\nmode: subagent\npermission:\n  edit: deny\n  bash: ask\n  webfetch: deny\n---\n\nOnly analyze code and suggest changes.\n```\n\n:::tip\nUse pattern matching for commands with arguments. `"grep *"` allows `grep pattern file.txt`, while `"grep"` alone would block it. Commands like `git status` work for default behavior but require explicit permission (like `"git status *"`) when arguments are passed.\n:::\n'},{title:"Rules",routePath:"/mimocode/rules",lang:"en",toc:[{text:"Initialize",id:"initialize",depth:2,charIndex:318},{text:"Example",id:"example",depth:2,charIndex:1250},{text:"Types",id:"types",depth:2,charIndex:1552},{text:"Project",id:"project",depth:3,charIndex:1673},{text:"Global",id:"global",depth:3,charIndex:1833},{text:"Claude Code Compatibility",id:"claude-code-compatibility",depth:3,charIndex:2106},{text:"Precedence",id:"precedence",depth:2,charIndex:2638},{text:"Custom Instructions",id:"custom-instructions",depth:2,charIndex:3204},{text:"Referencing External Files",id:"referencing-external-files",depth:2,charIndex:3726},{text:"Using mimocode.json",id:"using-mimocodejson",depth:3,charIndex:3881},{text:"Manual Instructions in AGENTS.md",id:"manual-instructions-in-agentsmd",depth:3,charIndex:3983}],frontmatter:{title:"Rules",description:"Set custom instructions for MiMo Code."},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode/rules.mdx",rawContent:'You can provide custom instructions to mimocode by creating an `AGENTS.md` file. This is similar to Cursor\'s rules. It contains instructions that will be included in the LLM\'s context to customize its behavior for your specific project.\n\n---\n\n## Initialize\n\nTo create a new `AGENTS.md` file, you can run the `/init` command in MiMo Code.\n\n:::tip\nYou should commit your project\'s `AGENTS.md` file to Git.\n:::\n\n`/init` scans the important files in your repo, may ask a couple of targeted questions when the codebase cannot answer them, and then creates or updates `AGENTS.md` with concise project-specific guidance.\n\nIt focuses on the things future agent sessions are most likely to need:\n\n- build, lint, and test commands\n- command order and focused verification steps when they matter\n- architecture and repo structure that are not obvious from filenames alone\n- project-specific conventions, setup quirks, and operational gotchas\n- references to existing instruction sources like Cursor or Copilot rules\n\nIf you already have an `AGENTS.md`, `/init` will improve it in place instead of blindly replacing it.\n\n---\n\n## Example\n\nYou can also just create this file manually. Here\'s an example of some things you can put into an `AGENTS.md` file.\n\n```markdown title="AGENTS.md"\n# SST v3 Monorepo Project\n\nThis is an SST v3 monorepo with TypeScript. The project uses bun workspaces for package management.\n\n## Project Structure\n\n- `packages/` - Contains all workspace packages (functions, core, web, etc.)\n- `infra/` - Infrastructure definitions split by service (storage.ts, api.ts, web.ts)\n- `sst.config.ts` - Main SST configuration with dynamic imports\n\n## Code Standards\n\n- Use TypeScript with strict mode enabled\n- Shared code goes in `packages/core/` with proper exports configuration\n- Functions go in `packages/functions/`\n- Infrastructure should be split into logical files in `infra/`\n\n## Monorepo Conventions\n\n- Import shared modules using workspace names: `@my-app/core/example`\n```\n\nWe are adding project-specific instructions here and this will be shared across your team.\n\n---\n\n## Types\n\nmimocode also supports reading the `AGENTS.md` file from multiple locations. And this serves different purposes.\n\n### Project\n\nPlace an `AGENTS.md` in your project root for project-specific rules. These only apply when you are working in this directory or its sub-directories.\n\n### Global\n\nYou can also have global rules in a `~/.config/mimocode/AGENTS.md` file. This gets applied across all mimocode sessions.\n\nSince this isn\'t committed to Git or shared with your team, we recommend using this to specify any personal rules that the LLM should follow.\n\n### Claude Code Compatibility\n\nFor users migrating from Claude Code, MiMo Code supports Claude Code\'s file conventions as fallbacks:\n\n- **Project rules**: `CLAUDE.md` in your project directory (used if no `AGENTS.md` exists)\n- **Global rules**: `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (used if no `~/.config/mimocode/AGENTS.md` exists)\n- **Skills**: `~/.claude/skills/` — see [Agent Skills](/mimocode/skills/) for details\n\nTo disable Claude Code compatibility, set one of these environment variables:\n\n```bash\nexport MIMOCODE_DISABLE_CLAUDE_CODE=1        # Disable all .claude support\nexport MIMOCODE_DISABLE_CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT=1 # Disable only ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md\nexport MIMOCODE_DISABLE_CLAUDE_CODE_SKILLS=1 # Disable only .claude/skills\n```\n\n---\n\n## Precedence\n\nWhen mimocode starts, it looks for rule files in this order:\n\n1. **Local files** by traversing up from the current directory (`AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`)\n2. **Global file** at `~/.config/mimocode/AGENTS.md`\n3. **Claude Code file** at `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (unless disabled)\n\nThe first matching file wins in each category. For example, if you have both `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, only `AGENTS.md` is used. Similarly, `~/.config/mimocode/AGENTS.md` takes precedence over `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`.\n\n---\n\n## Custom Instructions\n\nYou can specify custom instruction files in your `mimocode.json` or the global `~/.config/mimocode/mimocode.json`. Here\'s a practical example:\n\n```markdown title="AGENTS.md"\n# TypeScript Project Rules\n\n## External File Loading\n\nCRITICAL: When you encounter a file reference (e.g., @rules/general.md), use your Read tool to load it on a need-to-know basis. They\'re relevant to the SPECIFIC task at hand.\n\nInstructions:\n\n- Do NOT preemptively load all references - use lazy loading based on actual need\n- When loaded, treat content as mandatory instructions that override defaults\n- Follow references recursively when needed\n\n## Development Guidelines\n\nFor TypeScript code style and best practices: @docs/typescript-guidelines.md\nFor React component architecture and hooks patterns: @docs/react-patterns.md\nFor REST API design and error handling: @docs/api-standards.md\nFor testing strategies and coverage requirements: @test/testing-guidelines.md\n\n## General Guidelines\n\nRead the following file immediately as it\'s relevant to all workflows: @rules/general-guidelines.md.\n```\n\nThis approach allows you to:\n\n- Create modular, reusable rule files\n- Share rules across projects via symlinks or git submodules\n- Keep AGENTS.md concise while referencing detailed guidelines\n- Ensure mimocode loads files only when needed for the specific task\n\n:::tip\nFor monorepos or projects with shared standards, using `mimocode.json` with glob patterns (like `packages/*/AGENTS.md`) is more maintainable than manual instructions.\n:::\n'},{title:"Sessions & Context",routePath:"/mimocode/sessions",lang:"en",toc:[{text:"Session storage",id:"session-storage",depth:2,charIndex:372},{text:"Starting and resuming MiMo Code persists sessions, and how to resume, switch, and fork them while managing the context window."},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode/sessions.mdx",rawContent:'MiMo Code persists every conversation as a **session**, retaining its message history and metadata. You can close the terminal and resume your work later. This page covers how sessions are stored, resumed, switched, and forked, as well as how the context window is managed through compaction.\n\n---\n\n## Session storage\n\nSession data is saved in MiMo Code\'s data directory, grouped by working directory.\n\n```\n$MIMOCODE_HOME/\n├── config/    ← global config files\n├── data/      ← auth.json, session database\n├── state/\n└── cache/\n```\n\n`MIMOCODE_HOME` is an absolute path to a single profile root holding the `config/`, `data/`, `state/`, and `cache/` subdirectories; setting it overrides all four XDG base directories.\n\n:::warning\nDon\'t edit these files manually. Session state is managed by MiMo Code, and manual changes may make a session unrecoverable.\n:::\n\nWhen uninstalling, use `mimo uninstall --keep-data` to keep session data and snapshots. [Learn more](/mimocode/cli-subcommands).\n\n---\n\n## Starting and resuming sessions\n\nRunning MiMo Code creates a new session in the current directory.\n\n```bash\nmimo\n```\n\nYou can resume an existing session with the following flags:\n\n```bash\n# Resume the most recent session in the current directory\nmimo --continue\n\n# Resume a specific session by ID\nmimo --session \n```\n\n| Flag         | Short | Description                                             |\n| ------------ | ----- | ------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `--continue` | `-c`  | Continue the last session                               |\n| `--session`  | `-s`  | Session ID to continue                                  |\n| `--fork`     |       | Fork the session when continuing (use with `--continue` or `--session`) |\n\n:::note\n`--continue` and `--session` cannot both specify a new session source at once; `--fork` must be used together with one of them.\n:::\n\nSee the full list of CLI flags in [Command Line](/mimocode/cli-options).\n\n---\n\n## Switching sessions in the TUI\n\nInside the TUI, you can switch between sessions using slash commands:\n\n- **`/new`** (alias `/clear`) — start a new session, discarding the current context. Keybind `ctrl+x n`.\n- **`/sessions`** (aliases `/resume`, `/continue`) — list and switch between sessions. Keybind `ctrl+x l`.\n\nSee the full list of slash commands in [Slash Commands](/mimocode/slash-commands).\n\n---\n\n## Listing sessions\n\nOutside the TUI, use the `session` command to manage sessions.\n\n```bash\nmimo session list\n```\n\n| Flag          | Short | Description                                   |\n| ------------- | ----- | --------------------------------------------- |\n| `--max-count` | `-n`  | Limit to the most recent N sessions           |\n| `--format`    |       | Output format: table or json (default table)  |\n\n---\n\n## Context compaction\n\nEvery model has a limited **context window**. As a conversation grows, the MiMo Code CLI automatically compacts earlier messages as the context approaches that limit to free up token space. You can also trigger it manually at any time:\n\n```bash frame="none"\n/compact\n```\n\n`/compact` has the alias `/summarize` and the keybind `ctrl+x c`.\n\nCompaction is performed by a hidden [Compaction agent](/mimocode/agents#use-compaction) that compresses long context into a smaller summary.\n\n### Configuration\n\nYou can control context compaction behavior through the `compaction` option.\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "compaction": {\n    "auto": true,\n    "prune": true,\n    "reserved": 10000\n  }\n}\n```\n\n- `auto` - Automatically compact the session when context is full (default: `true`).\n- `prune` - Remove old tool outputs to save tokens (default: `true`).\n- `reserved` - Token buffer for compaction. Leaves enough window to avoid overflow during compaction.\n\nTo disable automatic compaction entirely, set the environment variable `MIMOCODE_DISABLE_AUTOCOMPACT=true`. [Learn more](/mimocode/cli-options).\n\n---\n\n## Forking sessions\n\nForking lets you derive an independent copy of a session to try new ideas without affecting the original. The two sessions are independent and don\'t affect each other.\n\n```bash\n# Fork from the most recent session and continue\nmimo --continue --fork\n\n# Fork from a specific session\nmimo --session  --fork\n```\n\nWhen running through the HTTP interface, you can also fork an existing session at a specific message..\n\n---\n\n## Exporting and importing\n\nYou can export a session to JSON for archiving, sharing, or bug reports.\n\n```bash\nmimo export [sessionID]\n```\n\nIf you don\'t provide a session ID, you\'ll be prompted to choose from the available sessions.\n\nYou can then import session data from a local file or a MiMo Code share link.\n\n```bash\nmimo import session.json\nmimo import https://opncd.ai/s/abc123\n```\n\n:::tip\nExported files may contain sensitive information (code, command output, paths, etc.). Review the contents before sharing.\n:::\n\nTo share a session live via a link, see [Share](/mimocode/share).\n'},{title:"Share",routePath:"/mimocode/share",lang:"en",toc:[{text:"How it works",id:"how-it-works",depth:2,charIndex:316},{text:"Sharing",id:"sharing",depth:2,charIndex:647},{text:"Manual (default)",id:"manual-default",depth:3,charIndex:823},{text:"Auto-share",id:"auto-share",depth:3,charIndex:1197},{text:"Disabled",id:"disabled",depth:3,charIndex:1522},{text:"Un-sharing",id:"un-sharing",depth:2,charIndex:1834},{text:"Privacy",id:"privacy",depth:2,charIndex:2080},{text:"Data retention",id:"data-retention",depth:3,charIndex:2242},{text:"Recommendations",id:"recommendations",depth:3,charIndex:2512},{text:"For enterprises",id:"for-enterprises",depth:2,charIndex:2916}],frontmatter:{title:"Share",description:"Share your MiMo Code conversations."},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode/share.mdx",rawContent:'MiMo Code\'s share feature allows you to create public links to your MiMo Code conversations, so you can collaborate with teammates or get help from others.\n\n:::note\nShared conversations are publicly accessible to anyone with the link.\n:::\n\n---\n\n## How it works\n\nWhen you share a conversation, MiMo Code:\n\n1. Creates a unique public URL for your session\n2. Syncs your conversation history to our servers\n3. Makes the conversation accessible via the shareable link — `opncd.ai/s/`\n\n---\n\n## Sharing\n\nMiMo Code supports three sharing modes that control how conversations are shared:\n\n---\n\n### Manual (default)\n\nBy default, MiMo Code uses manual sharing mode. Sessions are not shared automatically, but you can manually share them using the `/share` command:\n\n```\n/share\n```\n\nThis will generate a unique URL that\'ll be copied to your clipboard.\n\nTo explicitly set manual mode in your [config file](/mimocode/config-files):\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "share": "manual"\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n### Auto-share\n\nYou can enable automatic sharing for all new conversations by setting the `share` option to `"auto"` in your [config file](/mimocode/config-files):\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "share": "auto"\n}\n```\n\nWith auto-share enabled, every new conversation will automatically be shared and a link will be generated.\n\n---\n\n### Disabled\n\nYou can disable sharing entirely by setting the `share` option to `"disabled"` in your [config file](/mimocode/config-files):\n\n```json title="mimocode.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/config.json",\n  "share": "disabled"\n}\n```\n\nTo enforce this across your team for a given project, add it to the `mimocode.json` in your project and check into Git.\n\n---\n\n## Un-sharing\n\nTo stop sharing a conversation and remove it from public access:\n\n```\n/unshare\n```\n\nThis will remove the share link and delete the data related to the conversation.\n\n---\n\n## Privacy\n\nThere are a few things to keep in mind when sharing a conversation.\n\n---\n\n### Data retention\n\nShared conversations remain accessible until you explicitly unshare them. This\nincludes:\n\n- Full conversation history\n- All messages and responses\n- Session metadata\n\n---\n\n### Recommendations\n\n- Only share conversations that don\'t contain sensitive information.\n- Review conversation content before sharing.\n- Unshare conversations when collaboration is complete.\n- Avoid sharing conversations with proprietary code or confidential data.\n- For sensitive projects, disable sharing entirely.\n\n---\n\n## For enterprises\n\nFor enterprise deployments, the share feature can be:\n\n- **Disabled** entirely for security compliance\n- **Restricted** to users authenticated through SSO only\n- **Self-hosted** on your own infrastructure\n\nLearn more about using MiMo Code in your organization.\n'},{title:"Agent definitions"},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode/skills.mdx",rawContent:'Agent skills let MiMo Code discover reusable instructions from your repo or home directory.\nSkills are loaded on-demand via the native `skill` tool—agents see available skills and can load the full content when needed.\n\n---\n\n## Place files\n\nPut a `SKILL.md` inside a folder. The skill\'s name comes from its frontmatter—not the folder name—and `SKILL.md` files are discovered recursively, so you can nest them however you like.\n\nMiMo Code searches these locations:\n\n- Project config: `.mimocode/skills/**/SKILL.md` (the singular `skill/` folder also works)\n- Global config: `~/.config/mimocode/skills/**/SKILL.md`\n- Project compatibility dirs: `.claude`, `.agents`, `.codex`, and `.opencode`—each scanned at `skills/**/SKILL.md`\n- Global compatibility dirs: the same four folders under `~/` (e.g. `~/.claude/skills/**/SKILL.md`)\n\nAdd more sources with the `skills.paths` and `skills.urls` config options.\n\n---\n\n## Understand discovery\n\nFor project-local paths, MiMo Code walks up from your current working directory until it reaches the workspace root.\nAlong the way it loads any matching `skills/**/SKILL.md` (or `skill/**/SKILL.md`) in `.mimocode/`, and `skills/**/SKILL.md` in the `.claude`, `.agents`, `.codex`, and `.opencode` directories.\n\nGlobal definitions are also loaded from `~/.config/mimocode/` and from the same compatibility folders under your home directory (e.g. `~/.claude/skills/**/SKILL.md`).\n\nIf two skills resolve to the same `name`, the last one loaded wins and a warning is logged.\n\n---\n\n## Write frontmatter\n\nEach `SKILL.md` must start with YAML frontmatter.\nOnly these fields are read:\n\n- `name` (required)\n- `description` (required)\n- `hidden` (optional boolean—when `true`, the skill is loaded but kept out of the available-skills list)\n\nAny other frontmatter fields are ignored.\n\n---\n\n## Name your skill\n\nMiMo Code does not enforce a format on `name` or a length on `description`, but the `name` is how an agent references the skill, so keep it short and predictable. We recommend lowercase alphanumerics with single hyphens, e.g. `git-release`.\n\nKeep the `description` specific enough for the agent to choose the skill correctly—it is the only signal the agent sees before loading the full content.\n\n---\n\n## Use an example\n\nCreate `.mimocode/skills/git-release/SKILL.md` like this:\n\n```markdown\n---\nname: git-release\ndescription: Create consistent releases and changelogs\n---\n\n## What I do\n\n- Draft release notes from merged PRs\n- Propose a version bump\n- Provide a copy-pasteable `gh release create` command\n\n## When to use me\n\nUse this when you are preparing a tagged release.\nAsk clarifying questions if the target versioning scheme is unclear.\n```\n\n---\n\n## Recognize tool description\n\nMiMo Code lists available skills in the `skill` tool description.\nEach entry includes the skill name and description:\n\n```xml\n\n  \n    git-release\n    Create consistent releases and changelogs\n  \n\n```\n\nThe agent loads a skill by calling the tool:\n\n```\nskill({ name: "git-release" })\n```\n\n---\n\n## Configure permissions\n\nControl which skills agents can access using pattern-based permissions in `mimocode.json`:\n\n```json\n{\n  "permission": {\n    "skill": {\n      "*": "allow",\n      "pr-review": "allow",\n      "internal-*": "deny",\n      "experimental-*": "ask"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n| Permission | Behavior                                  |\n| ---------- | ----------------------------------------- |\n| `allow`    | Skill loads immediately                   |\n| `deny`     | Skill hidden from agent, access rejected  |\n| `ask`      | User prompted for approval before loading |\n\nPatterns support wildcards: `internal-*` matches `internal-docs`, `internal-tools`, etc.\n\n---\n\n## Override per agent\n\nGive specific agents different permissions than the global defaults.\n\n**For custom agents** (in agent frontmatter):\n\n```yaml\n---\npermission:\n  skill:\n    "documents-*": "allow"\n---\n```\n\n**For built-in agents** (in `mimocode.json`):\n\n```json\n{\n  "agent": {\n    "plan": {\n      "permission": {\n        "skill": {\n          "internal-*": "allow"\n        }\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Disable the skill tool\n\nCompletely disable skills for agents that shouldn\'t use them:\n\n**For custom agents**:\n\n```yaml\n---\ntools:\n  skill: false\n---\n```\n\n**For built-in agents**:\n\n```json\n{\n  "agent": {\n    "plan": {\n      "tools": {\n        "skill": false\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nWhen disabled, the `` section is omitted entirely.\n\n---\n\n## Troubleshoot loading\n\nIf a skill does not show up:\n\n1. Verify `SKILL.md` is spelled in all caps\n2. Check that frontmatter includes `name` and `description`\n3. Ensure skill names are unique—a duplicate `name` is silently overridden (last one loaded wins)\n4. Check permissions—skills with `deny` are hidden from agents\n'},{title:"Slash Commands",routePath:"/mimocode/slash-commands",lang:"en",toc:[{text:"connect",id:"connect",depth:3,charIndex:603},{text:"compact",id:"compact",depth:3,charIndex:798},{text:"details",id:"details",depth:3,charIndex:960},{text:"editor",id:"editor",depth:3,charIndex:1106},{text:"exit",id:"exit",depth:3,charIndex:1334},{text:"export",id:"export",depth:3,charIndex:1481},{text:"help",id:"help",depth:3,charIndex:1737},{text:"init",id:"init",depth:3,charIndex:1871},{text:"models",id:"models",depth:3,charIndex:2044},{text:"new",id:"new",depth:3,charIndex:2181},{text:"redo",id:"redo",depth:3,charIndex:2327},{text:"sessions",id:"sessions",depth:3,charIndex:2654},{text:"share",id:"share",depth:3,charIndex:2832},{text:"themes",id:"themes",depth:3,charIndex:2980},{text:"thinking",id:"thinking",depth:3,charIndex:3117},{text:"undo",id:"undo",depth:3,charIndex:3610},{text:"unshare",id:"unshare",depth:3,charIndex:3999}],frontmatter:{title:"Slash Commands",description:"Reference for MiMo Code's built-in slash commands."},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode/slash-commands.mdx",rawContent:'In addition to commands you define yourself, MiMo Code ships with a set of built-in slash commands. own."},version:"",_relativePath:"en/mimocode/themes.mdx",rawContent:'With MiMo Code you can select from one of several built-in themes, use a theme that adapts to your terminal theme, or define your own custom theme.\n\nBy default, MiMo Code uses our own `mimocode` theme.\n\n---\n\n## Terminal requirements\n\nFor themes to display correctly with their full color palette, your terminal must support **truecolor** (24-bit color). Most modern terminals support this by default, but you may need to enable it:\n\n- **Check support**: Run `echo $COLORTERM` - it should output `truecolor` or `24bit`\n- **Enable truecolor**: Set the environment variable `COLORTERM=truecolor` in your shell profile\n- **Terminal compatibility**: Ensure your terminal emulator supports 24-bit color (most modern terminals like iTerm2, Alacritty, Kitty, Windows Terminal, and recent versions of GNOME Terminal do)\n\nWithout truecolor support, themes may appear with reduced color accuracy or fall back to the nearest 256-color approximation.\n\n---\n\n## Built-in themes\n\nMiMo Code comes with several built-in themes.\n\n| Name                   | Description                                                                  |\n| ---------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `system`               | Adapts to your terminal’s background color                                   |\n| `tokyonight`           | Based on the [Tokyonight](https://github.com/folke/tokyonight.nvim) theme    |\n| `everforest`           | Based on the [Everforest](https://github.com/sainnhe/everforest) theme       |\n| `ayu`                  | Based on the [Ayu](https://github.com/ayu-theme) dark theme                  |\n| `catppuccin`           | Based on the [Catppuccin](https://github.com/catppuccin) theme               |\n| `catppuccin-macchiato` | Based on the [Catppuccin](https://github.com/catppuccin) theme               |\n| `gruvbox`              | Based on the [Gruvbox](https://github.com/morhetz/gruvbox) theme             |\n| `kanagawa`             | Based on the [Kanagawa](https://github.com/rebelot/kanagawa.nvim) theme      |\n| `nord`                 | Based on the [Nord](https://github.com/nordtheme/nord) theme                 |\n| `matrix`               | Hacker-style green on black theme                                            |\n| `one-dark`             | Based on the [Atom One](https://github.com/Th3Whit3Wolf/one-nvim) Dark theme |\n\nAnd more, we are constantly adding new themes.\n\n---\n\n## System theme\n\nThe `system` theme is designed to automatically adapt to your terminal\'s color scheme. Unlike traditional themes that use fixed colors, the _system_ theme:\n\n- **Generates gray scale**: Creates a custom gray scale based on your terminal\'s background color, ensuring optimal contrast.\n- **Uses ANSI colors**: Leverages standard ANSI colors (0-15) for syntax highlighting and UI elements, which respect your terminal\'s color palette.\n- **Preserves terminal defaults**: Uses `none` for text and background colors to maintain your terminal\'s native appearance.\n\nThe system theme is for users who:\n\n- Want MiMo Code to match their terminal\'s appearance\n- Use custom terminal color schemes\n- Prefer a consistent look across all terminal applications\n\n---\n\n## Using a theme\n\nYou can select a theme by bringing up the theme select with the `/theme` command. Or you can specify it in `tui.json`.\n\n```json title="tui.json" {3}\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/tui.json",\n  "theme": "tokyonight"\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Custom themes\n\nMiMo Code supports a flexible JSON-based theme system that allows users to create and customize themes easily.\n\n---\n\n### Hierarchy\n\nThemes are loaded from multiple directories in the following order where later directories override earlier ones:\n\n1. **Built-in themes** - These are embedded in the binary\n2. **User config directory** - Defined in `~/.config/mimocode/themes/*.json` or `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/mimocode/themes/*.json`\n3. **Project root directory** - Defined in the `/.mimocode/themes/*.json`\n4. **Current working directory** - Defined in `./.mimocode/themes/*.json`\n\nIf multiple directories contain a theme with the same name, the theme from the directory with higher priority will be used.\n\n---\n\n### Creating a theme\n\nTo create a custom theme, create a JSON file in one of the theme directories.\n\nFor user-wide themes:\n\n```bash no-frame\nmkdir -p ~/.config/mimocode/themes\nvim ~/.config/mimocode/themes/my-theme.json\n```\n\nAnd for project-specific themes.\n\n```bash no-frame\nmkdir -p .mimocode/themes\nvim .mimocode/themes/my-theme.json\n```\n\n---\n\n### JSON format\n\nThemes use a flexible JSON format with support for:\n\n- **Hex colors**: `"#ffffff"`\n- **ANSI colors**: `3` (0-255)\n- **Color references**: `"primary"` or custom definitions\n- **Dark/light variants**: `{"dark": "#000", "light": "#fff"}`\n- **No color**: `"none"` - Uses the terminal\'s default color or transparent\n\n---\n\n### Color definitions\n\nThe `defs` section is optional and it allows you to define reusable colors that can be referenced in the theme.\n\n---\n\n### Terminal defaults\n\nThe special value `"none"` can be used for any color to inherit the terminal\'s default color. This is particularly useful for creating themes that blend seamlessly with your terminal\'s color scheme:\n\n- `"text": "none"` - Uses terminal\'s default foreground color\n- `"background": "none"` - Uses terminal\'s default background color\n\n---\n\n### Example\n\nHere\'s an example of a custom theme:\n\n```json title="my-theme.json"\n{\n  "$schema": "https://mimo.xiaomi.com/mimocode/theme.json",\n  "defs": {\n    "nord0": "#2E3440",\n    "nord1": "#3B4252",\n    "nord2": "#434C5E",\n    "nord3": "#4C566A",\n    "nord4": "#D8DEE9",\n    "nord5": "#E5E9F0",\n    "nord6": "#ECEFF4",\n    "nord7": "#8FBCBB",\n    "nord8": "#88C0D0",\n    "nord9": "#81A1C1",\n    "nord10": "#5E81AC",\n    "nord11": "#BF616A",\n    "nord12": "#D08770",\n
